), it follows from the triangular inequality and Lemma
2.2
that for some
C>0
,

(2.66)

\|Y^{n}_{\tau_{n,j}}-\Xi_{\tau_{n,j}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},C_{X}}\exp\{-Cn^{2}\}+\|T-S_{j}\|^{\frac{l}{2}}_{L^{1}(\Omega)}+\left\||T-S_{j}|^{\tau}\right\|_{L^{8}(\Omega)}.

Denote

\Gamma^{n,j}_{t}:=\mathbb{E}_{t}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\int_{t\land\tau_{n,j}}^{\tau_{n,j}}2\alpha^{n,j}_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\Big\}\bigg],\ t\in[0,T].

In view of the fact that
\tau_{n,j}\leq T_{n},
by the procedure leading to (
2.48
), there exists
C^{\diamond}>0
such that for all
n\geq|x|,

(2.67)

\esssup\limits_{\omega\in\Omega,t\in[0,T]}|\Gamma^{n,j}_{t\wedge\tau_{n,j}}|\leq\exp\left\{C^{\diamond}n^{\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau}}\right\},\ \text{ for all }j\geq 1.

By (
2.62
) we have for
t\in[0,T]
,

(2.68)

\|\delta Y^{n,j}_{t\wedge\tau_{n,j}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}=\|Y^{n}_{t\wedge\tau_{n,j}}-Y^{n,j}_{t\wedge\tau_{n,j}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq\esssup\limits_{\omega\in\Omega,t\in[0,T]}|\Gamma^{n,j}_{t\wedge\tau_{n,j}}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|Y^{n}_{\tau_{n,j}}-\Xi_{\tau_{n,j}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Denote
C_{n}:=\exp\left\{\frac{C^{\diamond}}{2}n^{\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau}}\right\}
. While by Remark
2.3
,
\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau}<2.
Thus, by (
2.66
), (
2.67
), and (
2.68
), there exists
C^{\star}>0
such that

(2.69)

\|\delta Y^{n,j}_{t\wedge\tau_{n,j}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},C_{X}}\exp\left\{-C^{\star}n^{2}\right\}+C_{n}\left(\|T-S_{j}\|^{\frac{l}{2}}_{L^{1}(\Omega)}+\left\||T-S_{j}|^{\tau}\right\|_{L^{8}(\Omega)}\right).

(ii)
In this step, we estimate
\|Y_{t\wedge\tau_{n,j}}^{n}-Y_{t}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}
and then
\|Y_{t\wedge\tau_{n,j}}^{n,j}-Y_{t}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}
. Noting that
[
48
, Proposition 4.2]
yields the uniform boundedness of
\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y^{n}\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[0,T_{n}]}\right]
, we get

(2.70)

\|Y^{n}_{t\land\tau_{n,j}}-Y^{n}_{t\land T_{n}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{T_{n}\neq\tau_{n,j}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{n}\left\|\|Y^{n}\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T_{n}]}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},C_{X}}\mathbb{P}\left\{T\neq S_{j}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Moreover, by the estimate for the
\mathfrak{H}_{p,q}
norm of
(Y,Z)
established in
[
48
, Corollary 4.1]
, we have
\left\|\|Y\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},C_{X}}1
(note that by Theorem
1

(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{H}_{p,q}(0,T)
for every
q>1
). By the proof of Lemma
2.2
, there exists
\hat{C}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}\{T_{n}<T\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},C_{X}}\exp\{-\hat{C}n^{2}\}.

Thus, by (
2.53
), the following inequality holds for some constant
\check{C}>0,

(2.71)

\displaystyle\|Y^{n}_{t\land T_{n}}-Y_{t}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\|Y^{n}_{t\land T_{n}}-Y_{t\land T_{n}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}+\mathbb{P}\left\{T\neq T_{n}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\|Y\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},C_{X}}\exp\left\{-\check{C}n^{2}\right\}+\exp\left\{-\hat{C}n^{2}\right\}.

Combining (
2.69
), (
2.70
), and (
2.71
) with the assumption
\mathbb{P}\{S_{j}\neq T\}\leq p_{j}
with
p_{j}\downarrow 0
, we get that for every
\delta>0,
there exists an
n_{0}\geq 1
such that for every
n\geq n_{0}
, there exists an
I_{n}\geq 1
depending on
C_{n}
and
\{p_{j}\}_{j\geq 1}
such that

(2.72)

\|Y^{n,j}_{t\land\tau_{n,j}}-Y_{t}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}<\frac{\delta}{2},\text{ for all }j\geq I_{n}.

(iii)
Finally, we prove the desired estimation (
2.61
). Fix
j\geq 1
. Since
X
is bounded on
[0,S_{j}]
, we can find an
n\geq 1
such that
\tau_{n,j}=S_{j}
, and hence
Y^{n,j}=Y^{S_{j}}
when
n
is sufficiently large. In view of (
2.72
), to calculate the difference between
Y^{n,j}
and
Y^{S_{j}}
, it suffices to establish a uniform (in
j
) estimate for

\widetilde{\delta Y}^{n,j}:=Y^{n+1,j}-Y^{n,j}.

Following the same argument leading to (
2.68
), there exist constants
\tilde{C},C^{\prime}>0
such that

(2.73)

\|\widetilde{\delta Y}^{n,j}_{t\land\tau_{n,j}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},C_{X}}\exp\left\{\tilde{C}n^{\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau}}\right\}\exp\left\{-C^{\prime}n^{2}\right\}.

Notice that the right-hand side of (
2.73
) is independent of
j\geq 1
. Since
\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau}<2
, for every
\delta>0
there exists an integer
n_{1}\geq 1
such that

(2.74)

\sum_{n\geq n_{1}}\|\widetilde{\delta Y}^{n,j}_{t\land\tau_{n,j}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\frac{\delta}{4}.

Also, by the moments estimate
[
48
, Proposition 4.3]
and Lemma
2.2
, for each
m\geq 1
, we have for
0<C^{*}<C^{\prime}

\sum_{n\geq m}\|Y^{n+1,j}_{t\land\tau_{n+1,j}}-Y^{n+1,j}_{t\land\tau_{n,j}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{C_{X}}\sum_{n\geq m}\exp\left\{-C^{*}n^{2}\right\}.

Then, for each
\delta>0
, there exists
n_{2}\geq 1
such that

(2.75)

\sum_{n\geq n_{2}}\|Y^{n+1,j}_{t\land\tau_{n+1,j}}-Y^{n+1,j}_{t\land\tau_{n,j}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq\frac{\delta}{4}.

Combining (
2.74
) and (
2.75
), we have

\sum_{n\geq n_{1}\vee n_{2}}\|Y^{n+1,j}_{t\land\tau_{n+1,j}}-Y^{n,j}_{t\land\tau_{n,j}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq\frac{\delta}{2},

and hence by Fatou’s Lemma and the fact that
\lim_{n}Y^{n,j}=Y^{S_{j}}
, we have for all
j\geq 1,n\geq n_{1}\vee n_{2}
,

(2.76)

\|Y^{n,j}_{t\land\tau_{n,j}}-Y^{S_{j}}_{t\land S_{j}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}.

Combining (
2.72
) and (
2.76
), for every
\delta>0
, letting
J_{\delta}:=I_{n_{0}\vee n_{1}\vee n_{2}}
we have for every
j\geq J_{\delta}
,

\|Y^{S_{j}}_{t\land S_{j}}-Y_{t}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq\delta.

Step 2.
Now we consider the case without assuming
\mathbb{P}\{S_{j}\neq T\}\leq p_{j}
with
p_{j}\downarrow 0
. That is, we assume
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\{S_{j}=T\}<1.

The estimates (
2.69
), (
2.71
), and (
2.76
) still hold. Hence, for every
\delta>0,
there exists
n^{\prime}\geq 1
such that

(2.77)

\limsup_{j\rightarrow\infty}\|Y^{n^{\prime}}_{t\land\tau_{n^{\prime},j}}-Y^{n^{\prime},j}_{t\land\tau_{n^{\prime},j}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}+\|Y_{t}-Y^{n^{\prime}}_{t\land T_{n^{\prime}}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}+\limsup_{j\rightarrow\infty}\|Y^{n^{\prime},j}_{t\land\tau_{n^{\prime},j}}-Y^{S_{j}}_{t\land S_{j}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq\delta.

In addition, since
\|\|Y^{n^{\prime}}\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}<\infty
, by the dominated convergence theorem, we have

(2.78)

\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\|Y^{n^{\prime}}_{t\land T_{n^{\prime}}}-Y^{n^{\prime}}_{t\land\tau_{n^{\prime},j}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\|Y^{n^{\prime}}_{t\land\tau_{n^{\prime},j}}-Y^{n^{\prime}}_{t\land T_{n^{\prime}}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}=0,

Then, by (
2.77
) and (
2.78
), we have
\limsup_{j\rightarrow\infty}\|Y_{t}-Y^{S_{j}}_{t\land S_{j}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}<\delta
for every
\delta>0,
which implies the
L^{1}(\Omega)
-convergence of
Y^{S_{j}}_{S_{j}\land t}\rightarrow Y_{t}
. This completes the proof.
∎

In Theorem
1
, we get the existence of the solution
(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{H}_{p,q}(0,T)
to (
1.1
). Let
S
be a stopping time such that
X
is bounded on
[0,S]
. The following result shows that the solution restricted on the random interval
[0,S]
belongs to
\mathfrak{B}_{p,q}(0,T)
if and only if
Y
is bounded on
[0,S]
.

Proposition 2.3
.

Assume
(A0)
,
(T)
, and
(A1)
are satisfied. Suppose
(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{H}_{p,q}(0,T)
is a solution to (
1.1
), with
q>1
. Let
S
be a stopping time not greater than
T
such that
X
is bounded on
[0,S]
. Then,
(\bar{Y}_{t},\bar{Z}_{t}):=(Y_{t\land S},Z_{t}\mathbf{1}_{[0,S]}(t))\in\bigcap_{q^{\prime}\geq 1}\mathfrak{B}_{p,q^{\prime}}(0,T)
if
\bar{Y}
is bounded. Moreover,

(\bar{Y},\bar{Z})\in\mathfrak{B}_{p,1}(0,T)
only if
\bar{Y}
is bounded.

Proof.

Assume
\bar{Y}
is a bounded process. For
j\geq 1,
denote

S_{j}:=\inf\Big\{t>0;\|Y\|_{p\text{-var};[0,t]}+\int_{0}^{t}|Z_{r}|^{2}dr=j\Big\}\land S.

Then, we have
S_{j}\uparrow S
as
j\rightarrow\infty
and
(\bar{Y}^{j},\bar{Z}^{j})\in\mathfrak{B}_{p,q}(0,T)
, where

(\bar{Y}^{j}_{t},\bar{Z}^{j}_{t}):=(Y_{t\land S_{j}},Z_{t}\mathbf{1}_{[0,S_{j}]}(t)),\ t\in[0,T].

Moreover, by
[
48
, Proposition 4.1]
,
(\bar{Y}^{j},\bar{Z}^{j})
is the unique solution in
\bigcap_{q^{\prime}\geq 1}\mathfrak{B}_{p,q^{\prime}}(0,T)
to

\begin{cases}d\bar{Y}^{j}_{t}=-f(t,X_{t},\bar{Y}^{j}_{t},\bar{Z}^{j}_{t})dt-\sum_{i=1}^{M}g_{i}(\bar{Y}^{j}_{t})\eta_{i}(dt,X_{t})+\bar{Z}^{j}_{t}dW_{t},~t\in[0,S_{j}],\\[5.0pt]
\bar{Y}^{j}_{S_{j}}=Y_{S_{j}},\end{cases}

noting that
[
48
, Assumption (H2)]
is satisfied for every
k=q^{\prime}\geq 1
, since
m_{p,q^{\prime}}(X;[0,T])<\infty
for every
q^{\prime}\geq 1
by Lemma
2.1
. Note that
X_{t\land S_{j}}
and
Y_{S_{j}}
are bounded uniformly in
j\geq 1
since
S_{j}\leq S
. By the same proof of Lemma
2.4
, we can show

\sup_{j\geq 1}\|(\bar{Y}^{j},\bar{Z}^{j})\|_{p,q^{\prime};[0,T]}<\infty
for all
q^{\prime}\geq 1
, which together with Fatou’s lemma implies
\|(\bar{Y},\bar{Z})\|_{p,q^{\prime};[0,T]}<\infty
for all
q^{\prime}\geq 1
.

On the other hand,
assuming
(\bar{Y},\bar{Z})\in\mathfrak{B}_{p,1}(0,T)
, it follows directly from (
1.5
) that
\bar{Y}
is bounded.
∎

2.2.
The uniqueness

Let
X^{t,x}
solve the SDE

(2.79)

X^{t,x}_{s}=x+\int_{t}^{s}b(X^{t,x}_{r})dr+\int_{t}^{s}\sigma(X^{t,x}_{r})dW_{r},\ (s,x)\in[t,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},

where we stress that
b(\cdot)
and
\sigma(\cdot)
are independent of the time variable, and consider the following Markovian type BSDE:

(2.80)

Y_{s}=h(X_{T}^{t,x})+\int_{s}^{T}f(r,X^{t,x}_{r},Y_{r},Z_{r})dr+\sum_{i=1}^{M}\int_{s}^{T}g_{i}(Y_{r})\eta_{i}(dr,X^{t,x}_{r})-\int_{s}^{T}Z_{r}dW_{r},\ s\in[t,T].

The uniqueness of the solution to Eq. (
2.80
) is stated below:

Theorem 2
.

Consider the forward-backward SDE system (
2.79
)-(
2.80
) and assume
(A0)
and
(A1)
. Suppose that the function
h(x)
in (
2.80
) belongs to
C^{\mathrm{Lip}}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{N})
. Assume further that for every
n\geq 1
, the set of regular points

(2.81)

\Lambda_{n}:=\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d};|x|=n\text{ and }\mathbb{P}\left\{T^{0,x}_{n}>0\right\}=0\right\}\text{ is closed,}

where
T^{t,x}_{n}:=T\land\inf\left\{s>t;|X^{t,x}_{s}|>n\right\}
. Then, the following two statements hold.

(i)

Assume
(\widetilde{Y}^{t,x},\widetilde{Z}^{t,x})
and
(\widehat{Y}^{t,x},\widehat{Z}^{t,x})
are two solutions in
\mathfrak{H}_{p,q}(t,T)
of Eq. (
2.80
) with
q>1
, satisfying

(2.82)

|\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{s}|\vee|\widehat{Y}^{t,x}_{s}|\leq C_{t,x}\left(1+|X^{t,x}_{s}|^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}\right)\text{ a.s., for all }s\in[t,T],

for some constant
C_{t,x}>0
. Then
(\widetilde{Y}^{t,x},\widetilde{Z}^{t,x})=(\widehat{Y}^{t,x},\widehat{Z}^{t,x})
.

(ii)

Let
(Y^{t,x},Z^{t,x})\in\bigcap_{v>1}\mathfrak{H}_{p,v}(t,T)
be the solution of Eq. (
2.80
) obtained in Theorem
1
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
(t,x)
such that

|Y^{t,x}_{s}|\leq C\left(1+|X^{t,x}_{s}|^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}\right)\text{ a.s., for all }\,s\in[t,T].

Proof of Theorem
2
.

For simplicity, we assume that
N=1
and
f=0
. The proof can be adapted for the situation
N>1
and
f\neq 0
by using the same argument as in
Appendix C
The proof of Theorem
for the general case
of Theorem
1
. Let
(Y^{t,x},Z^{t,x})
be the solution of Eq. (
2.80
) obtained in Theorem
1
.

First, we prove part (i), the uniqueness of the solution. Let
(\widetilde{Y}^{t,x},\widetilde{Z}^{t,x})\in\mathfrak{H}_{p,q}(t,T)
be a solution to Eq. (
2.80
) for some
q>1.
Additionally, we assume
\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}
satisfies (
2.82
) and hence
\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}
is bounded on
[0,T^{t,x}_{n}]
. The uniqueness will be proved by showing that for every fixed
(t,x)
,
Y^{n;t,x}\rightarrow\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

By Proposition
2.3
we have

\big(\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{\cdot\land T^{t,x}_{n}},\widetilde{Z}^{t,x}_{\cdot}\mathbf{1}_{[t,T^{t,x}_{n}]}(\cdot)\big)\in\mathfrak{B}_{p,q}(t,T).
Moreover, by
[
48
, Proposition 4.1]
,
(\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}},\widetilde{Z}^{t,x}_{s}\mathbf{1}_{[t,T^{t,x}_{n}]}(s))
is the unique solution in
\mathfrak{B}_{p,q}(t,T)
to

\begin{cases}dY_{s}=-f(s,X^{t,x}_{s},Y_{s},Z_{s})ds-g(Y_{s})\eta(ds,X^{t,x}_{s})+Z_{s}dW_{s},~s\in[t,T^{t,x}_{n}],\\[3.0pt]
Y_{T^{t,x}_{n}}=\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}.\end{cases}

Furthermore, (
2.82
) and Lemma
2.1
imply that

\sup_{n\geq 1}\left\|\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}\right\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega)}<\infty,\text{ for every }q^{\prime}>1.

Combining the above estimate with
[
48
, Proposition 4.2]
, we obtain the following estimate,

\sup_{n\geq 1}\left\|\left(\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{\cdot\land T^{t,x}_{n}},\widetilde{Z}^{t,x}_{\cdot}\mathbf{1}_{[t,T^{t,x}_{n}]}(\cdot)\right)\right\|_{\mathfrak{H}_{p,q^{\prime}};[t,T]}<\infty\ \text{for every }q^{\prime}>1,

which indicates that the norms of the truncated processes
(\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{\cdot\land T^{t,x}_{n}},\widetilde{Z}^{t,x}_{\cdot}\mathbf{1}_{[t,T^{t,x}_{n}]}(\cdot))
are uniformly bounded in
n.
By Fatou’s Lemma, we get,

(\widetilde{Y}^{t,x},\widetilde{Z}^{t,x})\in\mathfrak{H}_{p,q^{\prime}}(t,T),\text{ for every }q^{\prime}>1,

which allows us to apply the regularity result in
[
48
, Corollary 4.2]
to the get the following estimate:

(2.83)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[|T-T^{t,x}_{n}|^{8\tau}\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(1+|x|^{\frac{2(\lambda+\beta)}{\varepsilon}\vee 2}+\|h(X^{t,x}_{T})\|^{2}_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}[h(X^{t,x}_{T})]\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[T^{t,x}_{n},T]}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},x}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[|T-T^{t,x}_{n}|\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}[h(X^{t,x}_{T})])\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[T^{t,x}_{n},T]}\right].

In addition, by Example
2.1
(A0) with
\Xi_{s}=h(X^{t,x}_{s\land T})
and
q=4
, we also have

(2.84)

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}[h(X^{t,x}_{T})]\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[T^{t,x}_{n},T]}\right]\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[|T-T^{t,x}_{n}|\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining (
2.83
) and (
2.84
), we get

(2.85)

\|h(X^{t,x}_{T})-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}=\|\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},x}\left\{\mathbb{E}[|T-T_{n}^{t,x}|]\right\}^{\frac{1}{8}}+\left\{\mathbb{E}[|T-T_{n}^{t,x}|]\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Furthermore, by Example
2.1
(A0) again, we have

(2.86)

\left\|h(X^{t,x}_{T})-h(X^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}})\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}\left\{\mathbb{E}[|T-T^{t,x}_{n}|]\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Moreover, by (
2.7
) with
q=2
there, there exists a constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that for all
n>|x|
,

(2.87)

\mathbb{E}\left[|T-T^{t,x}_{n}|\right]\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}\exp\Big\{-\frac{(n-|x|)^{2}}{C^{\prime}}\Big\}\leq\exp\Big\{\frac{|x|^{2}}{C^{\prime}}-\frac{n^{2}}{2C^{\prime}}\Big\}\lesssim_{x}\exp\Big\{-\frac{n^{2}}{2C^{\prime}}\Big\}.

Consequently, by (
2.85
), (
2.86
), and (
2.87
), there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(2.88)

\displaystyle\|Y^{n;t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\|h(X^{t,x}_{T})-h(X^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|h(X^{t,x}_{T})-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},x}\exp\left\{-Cn^{2}\right\}.

On the other hand, for
n>|x|
, by the definition of
T^{t,x}_{n}
, we have
\big\|X^{t,x}_{\cdot\land T^{t,x}_{n}}\big\|_{\infty;[t,T]}\leq n
. This together with condition (
2.82
) yields the following estimate for the terminal value:

\Big\|\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},t,x}\Big\|1+\big|X^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}\big|^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lesssim n^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}\text{ for all }n>|x|.

This estimate allows us to repeat the proof of Lemma
2.4
with the terms
T_{n},X_{\cdot},\Xi_{T_{n}},Y^{n}_{\cdot},Z^{n}_{\cdot}
replaced respectively by
T^{t,x}_{n},X^{t,x}_{\cdot}
,
\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}
,
\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{\cdot\land T^{t,x}_{n}}
,
\widetilde{Z}^{t,x}_{\cdot}\mathbf{1}_{[t,T^{t,x}_{n}]}(\cdot)
, and we get

(2.89)

m_{p,2}(\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{\cdot\land T^{t,x}_{n}};[t,T])\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},t,x}n^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}\text{ for all }n>|x|.

For
s\in[t,T]
, denote

\widetilde{\alpha}^{n}_{s}:=\frac{g(Y^{n;t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}})-g(\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}})}{Y^{n;t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}}\mathbf{1}_{\left\{Y^{n;t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}\neq 0\right\}}\ \text{ and }\widetilde{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}:=\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[\exp\left\{\int_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}^{T^{t,x}_{n}}2\widetilde{\alpha}^{n}_{r}\eta(dr,X^{t,x}_{r})\right\}\right].

Similar to (
2.34
), by (
2.89
) we have

m_{p,2}(\widetilde{\alpha}^{n};[t,T])\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},t,x}n^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}\text{ for all }n>|x|.

Therefore, by the calculation leading to (
2.48
), there exists a constant
C^{*}>0
such that

(2.90)

\esssup_{s\in[t,T],\omega\in\Omega}|\widetilde{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}|\leq\exp\left\{C^{*}n^{\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau}}\right\},\text{ for all }n>|x|.

Note that for
s\in[t,T]
,

\displaystyle Y^{n;t,x}_{s}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{s}=Y^{n;t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}+\int_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}^{T^{t,x}_{n}}\widetilde{\alpha}^{n}_{r}\left(Y^{n;t,x}_{r}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{r}\right)\eta(dr,X^{t,x}_{r})-\int_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}^{T^{t,x}_{n}}\left[Z^{n;t,x}_{r}-\widetilde{Z}^{t,x}_{r}\right]dW_{r}.

Then, similar to (
2.49
), by the product rule proved in
[
48
, Corollary 2.1]
we have

\|Y^{n;t,x}_{\cdot}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[t,T^{t,x}_{n}]}=\bigg\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot\land T^{t,x}_{n}}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\int_{\cdot\land T^{t,x}_{n}}^{T^{t,x}_{n}}\widetilde{\alpha}^{n}_{r}\eta(dr,X^{t,x}_{r})\Big\}\left(Y^{n;t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}\right)\bigg]\bigg\|_{\infty;[t,T^{t,x}_{n}]}.

Hence, by (
2.88
) and (
2.90
), there exists a constant
\widetilde{C}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y^{n;t,x}_{\cdot}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[t,T^{t,x}_{n}]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\{\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\Big|\widetilde{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}\Big|\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\{\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}\Big[\left|Y^{n;t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}\right|^{2}\Big]\bigg|\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},t,x}\exp\left\{\widetilde{C}n^{\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau}}-\frac{1}{\widetilde{C}}n^{2}\right\}.

Thus, by Remark
2.3
, the fact that
\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau}<2
implies that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y^{n;t,x}_{\cdot}-\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[t,T^{t,x}_{n}]}\right]=0.

On the other hand, by Theorem
1
, it follows that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y^{n;t,x}_{\cdot}-Y^{t,x}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[t,T^{t,x}_{n}]}\right]=0,

which gives
\widetilde{Y}^{t,x}=Y^{t,x}
.
Finally,
\widetilde{Z}^{t,x}=Z^{t,x}
follows from the following equality:

\int_{t}^{s}\left(\widetilde{Z}^{t,x}_{r}-Z^{t,x}_{r}\right)dW_{r}=0,\ s\in[t,T].

Now we prove part (ii), for which it suffices to construct a function
u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that

(2.91)

u(s,X^{t,x}_{s})=Y^{t,x}_{s}\text{ a.s., for }s\in[t,T]~\text{ and }\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|u(t,x)|\lesssim 1+|x|^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}.

For
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},
denote
T^{t,x}_{n}:=\inf\{s>t;|X^{t,x}_{s}|>n\}\land T
for
n\geq 1
. Then, by
[
48
, Proposition 4.1]
, the equation

\begin{cases}dY_{s}=-f(s,X^{t,x}_{s},Y_{s},Z_{s})ds-g(Y_{s})\eta(ds,X^{t,x}_{s})+Z_{s}dW_{s},\quad s\in[t,T^{t,x}_{n}],\\
Y_{T^{t,x}_{n}}=h(X^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}),\end{cases}

has a unique solution in
\mathfrak{B}_{p,2}(t,T)
which is now denoted by
(Y^{n;t,x},Z^{n;t,x})
. Let
F_{n}(x)
be a smooth cutoff function such that
F_{n}(x)=1
for
|x|\leq n
and
F_{n}(x)=0
for
|x|>n+1
. Denote

\eta^{\delta}(t,x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho^{\delta}(t-s)\eta(s,x)ds,\ t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

where
\rho\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
is a mollifier and
\rho^{\delta}(t):=\rho(t/\delta)/\delta
. By the proof of
[
48
, Lemma A.2]
, we have
\lim_{\delta\downarrow 0}\|F_{n}(x)\eta(t,x)-F_{n}(x)\eta^{\delta}(t,x)\|_{\tau^{\prime},\lambda}=0
for every
\tau^{\prime}\in(0,\tau).
Choose
(\tau^{\prime},\lambda)
such that Assumption
(A1)
holds with
(\tau,\lambda)
replaced by
(\tau^{\prime},\lambda)
. By
[
48
, Proposition 4.1]
again, the following BSDE admits a unique solution in
\mathfrak{B}_{p,2}(t,T)
:

(2.92)

\begin{cases}dY_{s}=-f(s,X^{t,x}_{s},Y_{s},Z_{s})ds-g(Y_{s})\partial_{s}\eta^{\delta}(s,X^{t,x}_{s})ds+Z_{s}dW_{s},\quad s\in[t,T^{t,x}_{n}],\\
Y_{T^{t,x}_{n}}=h(X^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}),\end{cases}

which is denoted by
(Y^{n,\delta;t,x},Z^{n,\delta;t,x})
.
Clearly,

Y^{n,\delta;t,x}_{t}
and
Y^{n;t,x}_{t}
are both constants a.s., and we denote them by two deterministic functions

u^{n}(t,x):=Y^{n;t,x}_{t}\text{ and }u^{n,\delta}(t,x):=Y^{n,\delta;t,x}_{t},\text{ for }t\in[0,T],|x|\leq n.

Next, we will give the representation of
Y^{n,\delta;t,x}
by
u^{n,\delta}.
For any
\zeta\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
, denote by
\mathscr{Y}^{n,\delta;t,\zeta}
the unique solution to Eq. (
2.92
) with
X^{t,x}
replaced by
X^{t,\zeta}
, where
X^{t,\zeta}
is the unique solution of (
2.79
) with
x
replaced by
\zeta
. By Lemma
B.2
, there exists a progressively measurable modification of
Y^{n,\delta;t,x}_{s}(\omega)
(which we also denote by
Y^{n,\delta;t,x}_{s}(\omega)
, for simplicity) such that

\mathscr{Y}^{n,\delta;s,X^{t,x}_{s\wedge T^{t,x}_{n}}}_{s}=Y^{n,\delta;s,X^{t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}}_{s}\text{ a.s. for }s\in[t,T].

The uniqueness of the solution to (
2.92
) implies

\mathbf{1}_{[s<T^{t,x}_{n}]}\mathscr{Y}^{n,\delta;t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}=\mathbf{1}_{[s<T^{t,x}_{n}]}\mathscr{Y}^{n,\delta;s,X^{t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}}_{s}\text{ a.s. for }s\in[t,T].

Hence, we get

(2.93)

Y^{n,\delta;t,x}_{s}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &Y^{n,\delta;s,X^{t,x}_{s}}_{s},&s<T^{t,x}_{n};\\
&h(X^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}),&s\geq T^{t,x}_{n}\end{aligned}\right.\text{\ \ \ a.s. for }s\in[t,T],|x|\leq n.

By Lemma
B.2
,
u^{n,\delta}(s,X^{t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}})=Y^{n,\delta;s,X^{t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}_{n}}}_{s}
a.s., and thus (
2.93
) yields

(2.94)

Y^{n,\delta;t,x}_{s}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &u^{n,\delta}(s,X^{t,x}_{s}),\ &s<T^{t,x}_{n};\\
&h(X^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}),\ &s\geq T^{t,x}_{n}\end{aligned}\right.\text{\ \ \ a.s. for }s\in[t,T],|x|\leq n.

Noting
\lim_{\delta\downarrow 0}\|F_{n}(x)\eta(t,x)-F_{n}(x)\eta^{\delta}(t,x)\|_{\tau^{\prime},\lambda}=0,
by the stability result proved in
[
48
, Corollary 3.1]
, we have
u^{n,\delta}(t,x)\rightarrow u^{n}(t,x)
and
Y^{n,\delta;t,x}_{s}\rightarrow Y^{n;t,x}_{s}
for
s\in[t,T]
, as
\delta\rightarrow 0
. This convergence and (
2.94
) yield that

(2.95)

Y^{n;t,x}_{s}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &u^{n}(s,X^{t,x}_{s}),\ &s<T^{t,x}_{n};\\
&h(X^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}}),\ &s\geq T^{t,x}_{n}\end{aligned}\right.\text{\ \ \ a.s. for every }s\in[t,T],|x|\leq n.

On the other hand, by Theorem
1
,
Y^{n;t,x}_{s}\rightarrow Y^{t,x}_{s}
as
n\rightarrow\infty.
Hence,
Y^{t,x}_{t}=\lim_{n}Y^{n;t,x}_{t}
is a constant a.s. and we denote it by a deterministic function function
u(t,x):=Y^{t,x}_{t}=\lim u^{n}(t,x)
for
t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. As
n\rightarrow\infty,
noting that
\lim_{n}T^{t,x}_{n}=T,
by (
2.95
) and the fact that
\lim_{n}Y^{n;t,x}_{T}=\lim_{n}h(X^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{n}})=h(X^{t,x}_{T})=u(T,X^{t,x}_{T})
a.s., we get

(2.96)

Y^{t,x}_{s}=u(s,X^{t,x}_{s})\text{ a.s. for }s\in[t,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Noting that

\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y^{t,x}\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[t,T]}\right]\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}1+|x|^{\frac{2(\lambda+\beta)}{\varepsilon}\vee 2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\|X^{t,x}\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[t,T]}\right]\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}1+|x|^{\frac{2(\lambda+\beta)}{\varepsilon}\vee 2},

where the first inequality follows from
[
48
, Corollary 4.1]
and
the second one holds since
|h(X^{t,x}_{T})|\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}1+|x|+\|X^{t,x}\|_{p\text{-var};[t,T]}
, we have

(2.97)

|u(t,x)|=\|Y^{t,x}_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\left\|\|Y^{t,x}\|_{p\text{-var};[t,T]}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|h(X^{t,x}_{T})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}1+|x|^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}.

Combining (
2.96
) and (
2.97
), we get (
2.91
) and the proof of part (ii) is concluded.
∎

Remark 2.7
.

Condition (
2.81
) ensures the continuity of
x\mapsto T^{t,x}_{n}
, (see, e.g., Lemma
B.1
). Moreover, this continuity will be used to prove the Markov property for the forward-backward SDE (see Lemma
B.2
), which will play a critical role in the proof of Theorem
2
.

A sufficient condition for (
2.81
), e.g., is as follows. Denote
D:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d};|x|<n\}
. Assume the strict uniform ellipticity for
\sigma(x)\sigma^{\top}(x)
(equivalently,
\sigma^{\top}(x)\sigma(x)
) on
\bar{D}
, i.e., there exists
\rho>0
such that
a^{\top}\sigma(x)\sigma^{\top}(x)a\geq\rho|a|^{2}
for all
x\in\bar{D},a\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Then by Lions-Menaldi
[
41
, Lemma 2.4]
(see also Buckdahn-Nie
[
6
, Lemma 3.1]
),
\Lambda_{n}=D
and hence is closed.

By the proof of part (ii) in Theorem
2
, we have the following corollary.

Corollary 2.1
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2
, define
u(t,x):=Y^{t,x}_{t}
for
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then, we have
Y^{t,x}_{s}=u(s,X^{t,x}_{s})
a.s. for every
(s,x)\in[t,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},
and

|u(t,x)|\leq C\left(1+|x|^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}\right),\text{ for all }\,t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

where
C>0
is a constant independent of
(t,x)
.

2.3.
Linear BSDEs

In view of the importance of the linear case, for instance, in Pontryagin’s stochastic maximum principle (see the first motivation in Part I
[
48
, Section 1.1]
), we consider the following linear BSDE,

(2.98)

Y_{t}=\xi+\sum_{i=1}^{M}\int_{t}^{T}\alpha^{i}_{r}Y_{r}\eta_{i}(dr,X_{r})+\int_{t}^{T}\left(Z_{r}G_{r}+f_{r}\right)dr-\int_{t}^{T}Z_{r}dW_{r},\ t\in[0,T],

where
\xi\in\mathcal{F}_{T}
,
X_{t}
and

\alpha_{t}
are continuous adapted processes, and
f
and
G
are progressively measurable functions. We make the following (weaker) assumption for this linear case.

Assumption (A2)
.

Let
\tau,\lambda,p
be the parameters satisfying Assumption
(H0)
.

(1)

There exist
\rho,\chi,q\in(0,\infty)
such that

(2.99)

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left\{\rho\left(\|X\|^{\chi}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|X_{0}|^{\chi}\right)\right\}\right]<\infty;\ \mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left\{\|\alpha\|^{q}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\alpha_{0}|^{q}\right\}\right]<\infty.

(2)

There exists
\beta\geq 0
such that
\eta\in C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M})
.

(3)

(\tau,\lambda,\beta,q)
satisfies (recall that
N
is the dimension of
\xi
and
Y_{t}
)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \lambda+\beta<\chi\text{ and }q>\frac{\chi}{\chi-(\lambda+\beta)},\quad\text{if }N=1;\\
\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\tau}<\chi\text{ and }q>\frac{\chi}{\chi\tau-(\lambda+\beta)},\quad\text{if }N>1.\end{aligned}\right.

(4)

Denote

(2.100)

M_{t}:=\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}(G_{r})^{\top}dW_{r}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\right\}.

Assume that there exists
k>1
such that
(\xi,f)
satisfies

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\xi\right|^{k}M_{T}\right]\text{ and }\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{T}|f_{r}|dr\right|^{k}M_{T}\right]<\infty.

(5)

Assume
G
satisfies

(2.101)

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left\{\gamma\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\right\}\right]<\infty,

where
\gamma
is a positive constant such that

(2.102)

\gamma>\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\frac{\sqrt{v}}{\sqrt{k}-\sqrt{v}}\right)^{2}\vee\left(\frac{\sqrt{v}}{\sqrt{v}-1}\right)^{2}\right]~\text{ for some }v\in(1,k).

Remark 2.8
.

As an example, when
X=W
is a Brownian motion, we can choose
\chi=2
in Condition (
2.99
), and then
\lambda+\beta<2
(resp.
\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\tau}<2
) is required when
N=1
(resp. when
N>1
) in order to satisfy condition (3) in Assumption
(A2)
.

In
[
48
, Section 3.2]
, we studied the well-posedness of the linear BSDE (
2.98
) assuming that
\eta
,
\alpha
, and
\xi
are bounded. In this section, we will remove this boundedness restriction. To this end, we shall derive an explicit formula for the solution, which is known as the Feynman-Kac formula for the associated linear Young PDE. For simplicity of notation, we assume
M=1
without loss of generality throughout this section.

We first consider the case with a null drift, i.e.,
(f,G)=0
in (
2.98
). To get an explicit formula for the solution, it suffices to prove that the process

Y_{t}:=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{\top}\xi\right],t\in[0,T],

is a unique solution to (
2.98
), where
(\Gamma_{s}^{t},s\geq t)
uniquely solves the following Young differential equation,

(2.103)

\begin{cases}d\Gamma_{s}^{t}=(\alpha_{s})^{\top}\Gamma_{s}^{t}\eta(ds,X_{s}),\ s>t,\\
\Gamma_{t}^{t}=I_{N},\end{cases}

with
I_{N}
denoting an
N\times N
unit matrix. We briefly explain the idea here and the detailed argument will be provided in
2.3.
Linear BSDEs
of Theorem
3
afterwards.

We introduce the following property of
\Gamma
which plays a key role in the study of linear BSDEs.

Lemma 2.5
.

Assume
(A2)
holds. Then for each
t\in[0,T]
, there exists a unique solution
\Gamma^{t}_{\cdot}\in C^{\tau\text{-}\mathrm{var}}([t,T])
to (
2.103
). Furthermore, we have
\Gamma^{t}_{T}=\Gamma^{s}_{T}\Gamma^{t}_{s}
a.s. for
s\in[t,T]
.

Proof.

Eq. (
2.103
) can be written as, for
\nu\in[t,T],

(2.104)

\Gamma^{t}_{\nu}=I_{N}+\int_{t}^{\nu}(\alpha_{r})^{\top}\Gamma^{t}_{r}dM_{r},

where
M_{t}:=\int_{0}^{t}\eta(dr,X_{r})
, and the integral is Young integral. Then, by
[
38
, Proposition 7]
, almost surely, (
2.104
) admits a unique solution
\Gamma^{t}_{.}
in
C^{\tau\text{-var}}([t,T])
.
Fix
s\in[t,T]
and define

\hat{\Gamma}_{\nu}:=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Gamma^{t}_{\nu},\ \nu\in[t,s],\\
&\Gamma^{s}_{\nu}\Gamma^{t}_{s},\ \nu\in[s,T].\end{aligned}\right.

Clearly,
\hat{\Gamma}_{\nu}
satisfies (
2.104
) for
{\nu}\in[t,T]
. The uniqueness of the solution
implies
\hat{\Gamma}_{\nu}=\Gamma^{t}_{\nu}
a.s. for
\nu\in[t,T]
and the desired result follows when
\nu=T
.
∎

The uniqueness of the solution to (
2.98
) is a consequence of Itô’s formula. Indeed, given a solution
(Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime})
of (
2.98
), applying the product rule (
[
48
, Corollary 2.1]
) to
(\Gamma_{s}^{t})^{\top}Y^{\prime}_{s}
on
[t,T]
, we have
d((\Gamma_{s}^{t})^{\top}Y^{\prime}_{s})=(\Gamma_{s}^{t})^{\top}Z_{s}dW_{s}
, and hence

(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{\top}\xi=Y^{\prime}_{t}+\int_{t}^{T}(\Gamma_{s}^{t})^{\top}Z_{s}dW_{s},

which implies that
Y^{\prime}_{t}=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{\top}\xi\right].

To prove the existence, we construct a solution to Eq. (
2.98
).
For any fixed
t\in[0,T]
, by the product rule, clearly the inverse of
\Gamma^{t}_{r}
for any
r\in[t,T]
, denoted by
(\Gamma_{r}^{t})^{-1}
, exists. Moreover, the flow
(\Gamma_{\cdot}^{t})^{-1}
satisfies

(\Gamma_{r}^{t})^{-1}=I_{N}-\int_{t}^{r}(\Gamma_{s}^{t})^{-1}(\alpha_{s})^{\top}\eta(ds,X_{s}),~r\in[t,T].

In particular, when
r=T
we have

(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{-1}=I_{N}-\int_{t}^{T}(\Gamma_{s}^{t})^{-1}(\alpha_{s})^{\top}\eta(ds,X_{s}).

Denote
\tilde{\Gamma}^{t}_{s}:=((\Gamma^{t}_{s})^{\top})^{-1}
. Then

(2.105)

\tilde{\Gamma}^{t}_{T}=I_{N}-\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}\tilde{\Gamma}^{t}_{s}\eta(ds,X_{s}).

Multiplying (
2.105
) by
(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{\top}\xi
, we obtain (noting that
(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}=(\Gamma^{t}_{s})^{\top}(\Gamma^{s}_{T})^{\top}
by Lemma
2.5
)

\xi=(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{\top}\xi-\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}(\Gamma^{s}_{T})^{\top}\xi\eta(ds,X_{s}).

Taking the conditional expectation of the above equality, we have

(2.106)

\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\xi\right]=\mathbb{E}_{t}[\xi]+\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}(\Gamma^{s}_{T})^{\top}\xi\eta(ds,X_{s})\right].

Thus, assuming the following tower rule,

(2.107)

\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}(\Gamma^{s}_{T})^{\top}\xi\eta(ds,X_{s})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[(\Gamma^{s}_{T})^{\top}\xi\right]\eta(ds,X_{s})\right],

the equality (
2.106
) implies

(2.108)

\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\xi\right]=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\xi\right]+\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[(\Gamma^{s}_{T})^{\top}\xi\right]\eta(ds,X_{s})\right],

and hence
Y_{t}=\mathbb{E}_{t}[(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\xi]
is a solution to (
2.98
) by the martingale representation theorem.

As indicated above, one key step for the well-posedness is to validate the tower rule (
2.107
). Therefore, it is sufficient to verify the conditions of Lemma
A.2
, which requires an estimate on
\Gamma^{t}_{T}
.

Lemma 2.6
.

Assume
(A2)
holds and let
(\Gamma_{s}^{t},s\geq t)
be given by (
2.103
) with
t\in[0,T]
. Then we have for all
\kappa\geq 0
,

(2.109)

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Gamma^{0}_{\cdot}\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-}\mathrm{var};[0,T]}\right]\vee\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\Gamma^{0}_{\cdot})^{-1}\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-}\mathrm{var};[0,T]}\right]<\infty,

and

(2.110)

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T}\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-}\mathrm{var};[0,T]}\right]\vee\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-}\mathrm{var};[0,T]}\right]<\infty.

Proof.

We will only prove (
2.110
), and (
2.109
) can be shown in a similar way.

Case
N>1
.
We first prove
\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]<\infty
. Clearly (
2.103
) is equivalent to

(2.111)

\Gamma^{t}_{T}=I_{N}+\int_{t}^{T}(\alpha_{r})^{\top}\Gamma^{t}_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r}),~t\in[0,T].

Noting
\Gamma^{r}_{T}\Gamma^{t}_{r}=\Gamma^{t}_{T}
by Lemma
2.5
, we get the following equation of
(\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot})^{-1}
:

(2.112)

I_{N}=(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{-1}+\int_{t}^{T}(\alpha_{r})^{\top}(\Gamma^{r}_{T})^{-1}\eta(dr,X_{r}).

Since
(\Gamma^{r}_{T})^{-1}=\Gamma^{0}_{r}(\Gamma^{0}_{T})^{-1}
and
\Gamma^{0}_{\cdot}\in C^{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var}}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{N\times N})
a.s., we have
(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\in C^{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var}}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{N\times N})
a.s. Applying the estimate for nonlinear Young integrals (
[
48
, Proposition 2.1]
) to
\int_{\cdot}^{T}(\alpha_{r})^{\top}(\Gamma^{r}_{T})^{-1}\eta(dr,X_{r})
on the right-hand side of (
2.112
), we can get

(2.113)

\displaystyle\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[t,T]}\leq C

\displaystyle|T-t|^{\tau}\|\eta\|_{\tau,\lambda;\beta}\left(1+\|X\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|X_{0}|\right)^{\lambda+\beta}

\displaystyle\times\left(\|\alpha\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\alpha_{0}|\right)\left(1+\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[t,T]}\right),

where
C
is a positive constant depending on the parameters in
(A2)
.

Firstly we estimate
\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var}}
on small intervals.
Denote the length of small intervals by

(2.114)

\theta:=\left(2C\|\eta\|_{\tau,\lambda;\beta}\left(1+\|X\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|X_{0}|\right)^{\lambda+\beta}\left(\|\alpha\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\alpha_{0}|\right)\right)^{-\frac{1}{\tau}}\wedge T.

Then, we get by (
2.113
),

\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[t,T]}\leq 1\ \text{ and }\ \|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\infty;[t,T]}\leq\bar{C}:=1+\sqrt{N},~\text{ for }t\in[T-\theta,T],

where the second inequality is due to the fact
\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\infty;[t,T]}\leq\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[t,T]}+|I_{N}|
. Similarly, we have, for every
t\in[T-(n+1)\theta,T-n\theta]
(we stipulate
T-n\theta=0
if
T-n\theta<0
),

(2.115)

\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T-n\theta})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[t,T-n\theta]}\leq 1\ \text{ and }\ \|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T-n\theta})^{-1}\|_{\infty;[t,T-n\theta]}\leq\bar{C}.

We are ready to estimate
\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}
.
By (
2.115
) and the fact that
(\Gamma^{a}_{c})^{-1}=(\Gamma^{a}_{b})^{-1}(\Gamma^{b}_{c})^{-1}
for
a\leq b\leq c
, we have

(2.116)

\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\infty;[T-n\theta,T]}\leq{\bar{C}}^{n}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[T-(n+1)\theta,T]}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T-n\theta})^{-1}(\Gamma^{T-n\theta}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[T-(n+1)\theta,T-n\theta]}+\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[T-n\theta,T]}

\displaystyle\leq|(\Gamma^{T-n\theta}_{T})^{-1}|\cdot\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T-n\theta})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[T-(n+1)\theta,T-n\theta]}+\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[T-n\theta,T]}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}^{n}+\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[T-n\theta,T]},

where the last step follows from (
2.115
) and (
2.116
).
Multiplying the above inequality by
(\bar{C}-1)
recursively yields

(\bar{C}-1)\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[T-(n+1)\theta,T]}-\bar{C}^{n+1}\leq(\bar{C}-1)\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[T-n\theta,T]}-\bar{C}^{n}\leq\cdots\leq-1,

and then

\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\leq(\bar{C}^{\frac{T}{\theta}+1}-1)/(\bar{C}-1).

Recalling that
\theta
is given in (
2.114
), we can find a constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

(2.117)

\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\leq C^{\prime}\exp\Big\{C^{\prime}\Big(1+\|X\|^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\tau}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|X_{0}|^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\tau}}\Big)\Big(\|\alpha\|^{\frac{1}{\tau}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\alpha_{0}|^{\frac{1}{\tau}}\Big)\Big\},

and thus by Young’s inequality we have for some constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\Big\{C^{\prime\prime}\Big(\|X\|^{\frac{q(\lambda+\beta)}{q\tau-1}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|X_{0}|^{\frac{q(\lambda+\beta)}{q\tau-1}}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\|\alpha\|^{q}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\alpha_{0}|^{q}\right)\Big\}\right],

which is finite due to the condition (
2.99
) and the fact
\frac{q(\lambda+\beta)}{q\tau-1}<\chi
.

Secondly, we show that
\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T}\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]<\infty
. For any fixed
t\in[0,T]
, by (
2.105
), we have

(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{-1}=I_{N}-\int_{t}^{T}(\Gamma_{r}^{t})^{-1}(\alpha_{r})^{\top}\eta(dr,X_{r}),~t\in[0,T].

Left-multiplying both sides by
\Gamma_{T}^{t}
and noting that
\Gamma_{T}^{t}=\Gamma_{T}^{r}\Gamma_{r}^{t}
for
r\in[t,T]
, we get the equation which
\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot}
satisfies

\Gamma_{T}^{t}=I_{N}+\int_{t}^{T}\Gamma_{T}^{r}(\alpha_{r})^{\top}\eta(dr,X_{r}),~t\in[0,T].

Then, we can prove
\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T}\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]<\infty
in the same way as for
(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}
.

Case
N=1
.
Note that
\Gamma^{t}_{T}
and
(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{-1}
have explicit expressions respectively

\Gamma^{t}_{T}=\exp\Big\{\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\Big\}\ \text{ and }\ (\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{-1}=\exp\Big\{\int_{t}^{T}-\alpha_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\Big\},

which enables us to relax the condition for
N=1
(see condition (3) in Assumption
(A2)
).

By the mean value theorem, for any partition
\pi
on
[0,T]
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{[t_{i},t_{i+1}]\in\pi}\big|\Gamma_{T}^{t_{i}}-\Gamma_{T}^{t_{i+1}}\big|^{\frac{1}{\tau}}\leq\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\exp\left\{\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{s}^{T}\alpha_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\right\}\sum_{[t_{i},t_{i+1}]\in\pi}\left|\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\alpha_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\right|^{\frac{1}{\tau}},

and hence

(2.118)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot}\right\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\exp\Big\{\kappa\int_{s}^{T}\alpha_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\Big\}\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T}\alpha_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\Big\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\exp\Big\{2\kappa\int_{s}^{T}\alpha_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\Big\}\bigg]\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\Bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T}\alpha_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\Big\|^{2\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]\Bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By
[
48
, Proposition 2.1]
, there exists
C^{\prime}>0
such that

\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T}\alpha_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\Big\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\leq C^{\prime}\left(1+\|X\|^{\lambda+\beta}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|X_{0}|^{\lambda+\beta}\right)\left(\|\alpha\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\alpha_{0}|\right).

Plugging this into (
2.118
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot}\right\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left\{2\kappa C^{\prime}\left(1+\|X\|^{\lambda+\beta}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|X_{0}|^{\lambda+\beta}\right)\left(\|\alpha\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\alpha_{0}|\right)\right\}\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times(C^{\prime})^{\kappa}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1+\|X\|^{\lambda+\beta}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|X_{0}|^{\lambda+\beta}\right)^{2\kappa}\left(\|\alpha\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\alpha_{0}|\right)^{2\kappa}\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\kappa,C^{\prime}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\Big\{C^{\prime\prime}\left(\|X\|^{\frac{q(\lambda+\beta)}{q-1}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|X_{0}|^{\frac{q(\lambda+\beta)}{q-1}}\right)\Big\}\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left\{\|\alpha\|^{q}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\alpha_{0}|^{q}\right\}\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\times\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1+\|X\|^{\lambda+\beta}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|X_{0}|^{\lambda+\beta}\right)^{2\kappa}\left(\|\alpha\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\alpha_{0}|\right)^{2\kappa}\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

is finite, where the second inequality follows from Young’s inequality and Hölder’s inequality, and the last one is due to Assumption
(A2)
.

The finiteness of
\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{-1}\|^{\kappa}_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]
can be proven in the same way.
∎

Now we are ready to establish the well-posedness of the linear BSDE (
2.98
).

Theorem 3
.

Assume
(A2)
holds. Then, there exists a unique solution
(Y,Z)
to BSDE (
2.98
) in
\mathfrak{H}_{p,v}(0,T)
where
v
is a parameter appearing in (
2.102
).
Moreover,

(2.119)

Y_{t}=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\left((\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\xi+\int_{t}^{T}\Gamma^{t}_{s}f_{s}ds\right)M_{T}\right]M^{-1}_{t}\ \text{a.s.}~\text{ for }~t\in[0,T],

where
M_{t}
is defined in (
2.100
),
and
\Gamma^{t}_{\cdot}
is the unique solution to the following Young differential equation,

(2.120)

\Gamma^{t}_{s}=I_{N}+\sum_{i=1}^{M}\int_{t}^{s}(\alpha^{i}_{r})^{\top}\Gamma^{t}_{r}\eta_{i}(dr,X_{r}),\quad s\in[t,T],

with
I_{N}
denoting the
N\times N
identity matrix.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

We split our proof into four steps. In step 1, we establish some moment estimations. In step 2, we prove the well-posedness
for the case
f,G\equiv 0
. In step 3, we consider the case
G\equiv 0
, and in the step 4, we complete our proof for the general case.

Step 1.
Recall that
\Gamma^{t}_{\cdot}
solves Eq. (
2.103
), and
k>1
is a constant such that
\|\xi\|_{L^{k}(\Omega)}<\infty
. In this step, we shall prove the following estimates: for each
k^{\prime}\in(1,k)
,

(2.121)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{0})^{\top}\xi\right]\right\|^{k^{\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]<\infty,

(2.122)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|(\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot})^{\top}\xi\right\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}^{k^{\prime}}\right]<\infty,

and

(2.123)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot})^{\top}\xi\right]\right\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}^{k^{\prime}}\right]<\infty.

By the BDG inequality for
p
-variation and Doob’s maximal inequality, we have, for
1<k^{\prime}<k
,

(2.124)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{0})^{\top}\xi\right]\right\|^{k^{\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]\lesssim_{p,k^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|(\Gamma_{T}^{0})^{\top}\xi\right|^{k^{\prime}}\right].

Then, by (
2.124
), Hölder’s inequality, and Lemma
2.6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{0})^{\top}\xi\right]\right\|^{k^{\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]\lesssim_{p,k^{\prime}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\xi\right|^{k}\right]\right\}^{\frac{k^{\prime}}{k}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\left|(\Gamma_{T}^{0})^{\top}\right|^{\frac{kk^{\prime}}{k-k^{\prime}}}\Big]\Big\}^{\frac{k-k^{\prime}}{k}}<\infty,

which proves (
2.121
).

Noting
\frac{1}{\tau}<2<p
, by Lemma
2.6
, all moments of
\|(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{T})^{\top}\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}
are finite. In addition, since
\|\xi\|_{L^{k}(\Omega)}<\infty
and
k>k^{\prime}
, (
2.122
) follows directly from Hölder’s inequality.

Regarding (
2.123
), noting that
\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{\top}\xi\right]=((\Gamma^{0}_{t})^{-1})^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{0})^{\top}\xi\right]
, we have, for
1<k^{\prime}<k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot})^{\top}\xi\right]\right\|^{k^{\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|((\Gamma^{0}_{\cdot})^{-1})^{\top}\right\|^{k^{\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{0})^{\top}\xi\right]\right\|^{k^{\prime}}_{\infty;[0,T]}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|((\Gamma^{0}_{\cdot})^{-1})^{\top}\right\|^{k^{\prime}}_{\infty;[0,T]}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{0})^{\top}\xi\right]\right\|^{k^{\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\right],

the right-hand side of which is finite due to Hölder’s inequality, (
2.121
), and Lemma
2.6
.

Step 2.

In this step, we will prove the existence and uniqueness of the solution to (
2.98
) when
f,G\equiv 0
. By (
2.122
), for
1<k^{\prime}<k
we have (noting
\|B\|_{\infty;[0,T]}\leq\|B\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|B_{T}|
for
B\in C^{p\text{-var}}
),

(2.125)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|(\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot})^{\top}\xi\right\|_{\infty;[0,T]}^{k^{\prime}}\right]\vee\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|(\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot})^{\top}\xi\right\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}^{k^{\prime}}\right]<\infty;

and similarly by (
2.123
),

(2.126)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot})^{\top}\xi\right]\right\|_{\infty;[0,T]}^{k^{\prime}}\right]\vee\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot})^{\top}\xi\right]\right\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}^{k^{\prime}}\right]<\infty.

In view of (
2.99
), (
2.125
), and (
2.126
), by Lemma
A.2
we get

(2.127)

\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{r}(\Gamma_{T}^{r})^{\top}\xi\eta(dr,X_{r})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{r}\mathbb{E}_{r}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{r})^{\top}\xi\right]\eta(dr,X_{r})\right],

and thus (
2.108
) holds, i.e.,

(2.128)

\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\xi\right]=\mathbb{E}_{t}[\xi]+\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{r}\mathbb{E}_{r}\left[(\Gamma^{r}_{T})^{\top}\xi\right]\eta(dr,X_{r})\right].

By
[
48
, Proposition 2.1]
, we have, for
1<k^{*}<k^{\prime}<k,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{\top}\xi\right]\eta(dt,X_{t})\Big|^{k^{*}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\tau,\lambda,\beta,p,k^{*}}\|\eta\|_{\tau,\lambda;\beta}^{k^{*}}T^{k^{*}\tau}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left(1+\left\|X\right\|^{k^{*}(\lambda+\beta)}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|X_{0}|^{k^{*}(\lambda+\beta)}\right)\left(\|\alpha\|^{k^{*}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\alpha_{0}|^{k^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{\cdot})^{\top}\xi\right]\right\|^{k^{*}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+|\xi|^{k^{*}}\Big)\bigg]

is finite, which follows from Hölder’s inequality, (
2.123
), (
2.99
) and the fact
\|xy\|_{p\text{-var}}\leq\|x\|_{p\text{-var}}\|y\|_{\infty}+\|y\|_{p\text{-var}}\|x\|_{\infty}
.
Thus,

\Big\|\xi+\int_{0}^{T}\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{\top}\xi\right]\eta(dt,X_{t})\Big\|_{L^{k^{*}}(\Omega)}<\infty.

Then, by the martingale representation theorem (as same as the proof in
[
48
, Lemma 3.1]
), there exists a unique progressively measurable process
Z
such that

(2.129)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}|Z_{r}|^{2}dr\Big|^{\frac{k^{*}}{2}}\bigg]<\infty,

and for every
t\in[0,T]
,

(2.130)

\xi+\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{s})^{\top}\xi\right]\eta(ds,X_{s})-\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\xi+\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{s})^{\top}\xi\right]\eta(ds,X_{s})\right]=\int_{t}^{T}Z_{s}dW_{s}.

By (
2.128
) and (
2.130
), we obtain that
Y_{t}:=\mathbb{E}_{t}[(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{\top}\xi]
together with
Z_{t}
satisfies BSDE (
2.98
) with
f,G\equiv 0
. In addition, by (
2.123
) and (
2.129
), we have
(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{H}_{p,k^{*}}(0,T)
.

The uniqueness of the solution can be shown by Itô’s formula. Indeed, if
(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{H}_{p,k^{*}}(0,T)
is a solution of Eq. (
2.98
), applying the product rule (
[
48
, Corollary 2.1]
) to
(\Gamma_{s}^{t})^{\top}Y_{s}
on
[t,T]
and then taking conditional expectation, we have

Y_{t}=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{\top}\xi\right]+\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}(\Gamma^{t}_{r})^{\top}Z_{r}dW_{r}\right].

While by Lemma
2.6
(noting
\Gamma^{t}_{r}=(\Gamma^{r}_{T})^{-1}\Gamma^{t}_{T}
) and
\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\int_{0}^{T}|Z_{r}|^{2}dr\big|^{\frac{k^{*}}{2}}\big]<\infty
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\Big|\int_{t}^{T}|(\Gamma^{t}_{r})^{\top}Z_{r}|^{2}dr\Big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]<\infty.

Thus, we have
\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[\int_{t}^{T}(\Gamma^{t}_{r})^{\top}Z_{r}dW_{r}\big]=0
, and then
Y_{t}=\mathbb{E}_{t}[(\Gamma_{T}^{t})^{\top}\xi]
.

Step 3.

In this step, we will prove the well-posedness assuming
G\equiv 0
.

Recall that
\tilde{\Gamma}^{t}_{T}=((\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top})^{-1}
satisfies (
2.105
).
Multiplying (
2.105
) by
(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}(\xi+\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{\Gamma}^{s}_{T}f_{s}ds),
and using the equality
(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\tilde{\Gamma}^{s}_{T}=(\Gamma^{t}_{s})^{\top}
and the equality
\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}\tilde{\Gamma}^{t}_{s}\eta(ds,X_{s})=I_{N}-\tilde{\Gamma}^{t}_{T}
, we have

(2.131)

\displaystyle\xi+\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{\Gamma}^{s}_{T}f_{s}ds

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\xi+\int_{t}^{T}(\Gamma^{t}_{s})^{\top}f_{s}ds-\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}(\Gamma^{s}_{T})^{\top}\xi\eta(ds,X_{s})

\displaystyle+\left(I_{N}-(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\right)\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{\Gamma}^{s}_{T}f_{s}ds.

In addition, for
s\in[t,T],
since
(\Gamma^{s}_{T})^{\top}=\tilde{\Gamma}^{t}_{s}(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}
we have
d\left(I_{N}-(\Gamma^{s}_{T})^{\top}\right)=-d\tilde{\Gamma}^{t}_{s}(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}.
Then in view of (
2.105
) and integration by parts formula, we have

\displaystyle\left(I_{N}-(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\right)\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{\Gamma}^{s}_{T}f_{s}ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}\tilde{\Gamma}^{t}_{s}(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\Big(\int_{s}^{T}\tilde{\Gamma}^{r}_{T}f_{r}dr\Big)\eta(ds,X_{s})+\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{\Gamma}^{s}_{T}f_{s}ds-\int_{t}^{T}f_{s}ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}\Big(\int_{s}^{T}(\Gamma^{s}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big)\eta(ds,X_{s})+\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{\Gamma}^{s}_{T}f_{s}ds-\int_{t}^{T}f_{s}ds.

By the above equality and (
2.131
), we have

(2.132)

(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\xi+\int_{t}^{T}(\Gamma^{t}_{s})^{\top}f_{s}ds=\xi+\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}\Big((\Gamma^{s}_{T})^{\top}\xi+\int_{s}^{T}(\Gamma^{s}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big)\eta(ds,X_{s})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{s}ds.

In view of (
2.132
), if we have the following tower rule:

(2.133)

\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}\Big(\int_{s}^{T}(\Gamma^{s}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big)\eta(ds,X_{s})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{s}\mathbb{E}_{s}\Big[\int_{s}^{T}(\Gamma^{s}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big]\eta(ds,X_{s})\right],

then
Y_{t}:=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\xi+\int_{t}^{T}(\Gamma^{t}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\right]

satisfies

Y_{t}=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\xi+\int_{t}^{T}\alpha_{r}Y_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}dr\right],\quad t\in[0,T],

and by the same procedure as in step 2, for each
k^{*}\in(1,k)
,
there exists a progressively measurable process
Z
such that
(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{H}_{p,k^{*}}(0,T)
, and this pair uniquely solves Eq. (
2.98
) with
G\equiv 0
.

To complete the proof, it suffices to prove the tower rule (
2.133
). In order to apply Lemma
A.2
, we will establish estimates for the moments of the uniform norm (resp.
p
-variation norm) of
s\mapsto\int_{s}^{T}(\Gamma^{s}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr
(resp.
s\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{s}[\int_{s}^{T}(\Gamma^{s}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr]
).
Note that

(2.134)

\int_{s}^{T}(\Gamma^{s}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr=\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{s}\int_{s}^{T}(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr,\quad s\in[0,T].

We have

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T}(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}

\displaystyle\leq\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\int_{0}^{T}\left|(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}\right|dr+\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[0,T]}\int_{0}^{T}\left|(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}\right|dr

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[0,T]}\right)\left\|\Gamma^{0}_{\cdot}\right\|_{\infty;[0,T]}\int_{0}^{T}|f_{r}|dr

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+1\right)\left(\|\Gamma^{0}_{\cdot}\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+1\right)\int_{0}^{T}|f_{r}|dr;

and hence by Lemma
2.6
and the fact
\mathbb{E}[\big|\int_{0}^{T}|f_{r}|dr\big|^{k}]<\infty
, we have for
1<k^{\prime\prime}<k
,

(2.135)

\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T}(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big\|^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{\infty;[0,T]}\Big]\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T}(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big\|^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\Big]<\infty.

In addition, noting

\mathbb{E}_{s}\Big[\int_{s}^{T}(\Gamma^{s}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big]=\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{s}\left(\mathbb{E}_{s}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr-\int_{0}^{s}(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big]\right),

we have

(2.136)

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\Big[\int_{\cdot}^{T}(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big]\Big\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}

\displaystyle\leq\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big]\Big\|_{\infty;[0,T]}+\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[0,T]}\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big]\Big\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\int_{0}^{T}\left|(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}\right|dr+\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[0,T]}\int_{0}^{T}\left|(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}\right|dr.

Thus, by (
2.136
), Hölder’s inequality, and the BDG inequality for
p
-variation, we have for
1<k^{\prime\prime}<k^{\prime}<k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\Big[\int_{\cdot}^{T}(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big]\Big\|^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big]\Big\|^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{\infty;[0,T]}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{\infty;[0,T]}\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big]\Big\|^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]

(2.137)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}+\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{\infty;[0,T]}\right)\Big|\int_{0}^{T}(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big|^{k^{\prime\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p,k^{\prime}}\bigg(\left\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|^{\frac{k^{\prime\prime}k^{\prime}}{k^{\prime}-k^{\prime\prime}}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\Big]\right\}^{\frac{k^{\prime}-k^{\prime\prime}}{k^{\prime}}}+\left\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|^{\frac{k^{\prime\prime}k^{\prime}}{k^{\prime}-k^{\prime\prime}}}_{\infty;[0,T]}\Big]\right\}^{\frac{k^{\prime}-k^{\prime\prime}}{k^{\prime}}}\bigg)\left\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big|^{k^{\prime}}\Big]\right\}^{\frac{k^{\prime\prime}}{k^{\prime}}}.

We claim that the right-hand side of the above inequality is finite. Indeed, by Lemma
2.6
, we have

(2.138)

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|^{\frac{k^{\prime\prime}k^{\prime}}{k^{\prime}-k^{\prime\prime}}}_{\infty;[0,T]}\right]\lesssim 1+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}_{\cdot}\|^{\frac{k^{\prime\prime}k^{\prime}}{k^{\prime}-k^{\prime\prime}}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]<\infty,

and

(2.139)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}(\Gamma^{0}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big|^{k^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Gamma^{0}_{\cdot}\right\|^{k^{\prime}}_{\infty;[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{T}|f_{r}|dr\Big|^{k^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\left\|\Gamma^{0}_{\cdot}\right\|^{\frac{kk^{\prime}}{k-k^{\prime}}}_{\infty;[0,T]}\Big]\right\}^{\frac{k-k^{\prime}}{k}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\left|f_{r}\right|dr\Big|^{k}\Big]\right\}^{\frac{k^{\prime}}{k}}<\infty.

Combining (
2.137
), (
2.138
), and (
2.139
), we get

(2.140)

\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\Big[\int_{\cdot}^{T}(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big]\Big\|^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{\infty;[0,T]}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\Big[\int_{\cdot}^{T}(\Gamma^{\cdot}_{r})^{\top}f_{r}dr\Big]\Big\|^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{p\text{-var};[0,T]}\right]<\infty.

Now, the desired tower rule (
2.133
) follows from Lemma
A.2
in view of (
2.99
), (
2.135
), and (
2.140
).

Step 4.

In this last step, we will prove the well-posedness for (
2.98
).

Denote
\tilde{W}_{t}:=W_{t}-\int_{0}^{t}G_{r}dr
. Then, by the Girsanov theorem (see, e.g.,
[
36
, Theorem 3.5.1]
),
\tilde{W}
is a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion on
(\Omega,(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
, where
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
is defined by

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}(A):=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{1}_{A}M_{T}\right],\quad A\in\mathcal{F}_{T},

with
M
being defined in (
2.100
). Hence, Eq. (
2.98
) can be rewritten as

(2.141)

Y_{t}=\xi+\sum_{i=1}^{M}\int_{t}^{T}\alpha^{i}_{r}Y_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}dr-\int_{t}^{T}Z_{r}d\tilde{W}_{r},\quad t\in[0,T].

Let
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}
be the expectation under probability
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
and denote
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{t}[\cdot]:=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\cdot|\mathcal{F}_{t}]
. Denote, for
k^{*}>1
,

\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,k^{*}}(0,T):=\left\{(y,z)\in\mathfrak{B}([0,T]):\ \|(y,z)\|_{\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,k^{*}};[0,T]}<\infty\right\},

where
\|(y,z)\|_{\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,k^{*}};[0,T]}
is defined by (
1.4
) with
\mathbb{E}[\cdot]
replaced by
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\cdot]
. Then, noting that
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\xi|^{k}\right]<\infty
and
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{T}|f_{r}|dr\right|^{k}\right]<\infty
, by Step 3, we see that

(2.142)

Y_{t}:=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{t}\left[(\Gamma^{t}_{T})^{\top}\xi+\int_{t}^{T}(\Gamma^{t}_{s})^{\top}f_{s}ds\right],\quad t\in[0,T],

together with some process
Z

uniquely solves (
2.141
) in the space
\bigcup_{1\leq k^{*}<k}\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,k^{*}}(0,T)
with

(Y,Z)\in\bigcap_{1\leq k^{*}<k}\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,k^{*}}(0,T)
, and hence
(Y,Z)
satisfies Eq. (
2.98
). Then, the Feynman-Kac representation (
2.119
) follows from applying Bayes’s rule (see, e.g.,
[
36
, Lemma 3.5.3]
) to (
2.142
).

To complete the proof, we still need to show that
(Y,Z)
is the unique solution in the space
\mathfrak{H}_{p,v}(0,T)
, where
v\in(1,k)
is the parameter in the condition (
2.102
).

We first prove that
(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{H}_{p,v}(0,T)
, for which it suffices to verify that
\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,k^{\prime}}(0,T)\subset\mathfrak{H}_{p,v}(0,T)
for
k^{\prime}\in(1,k)
sufficiently close to
k
.
By (
2.102
), there exists
k^{\prime}\in(v,k)
such that

(2.143)

\gamma>\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{v}}{\sqrt{k^{\prime}}-\sqrt{v}}\Big)^{2}.

For any random variable
\zeta\in\mathcal{F}_{T}
with
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[|\zeta|^{k^{\prime}}]<\infty
, by Hölder’s inequality we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\zeta|^{v}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[|\zeta|^{v}\exp\Big\{\frac{v}{k^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{T}(G_{r})^{\top}dW_{r}-\frac{v}{2k^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr-\frac{v}{k^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{T}(G_{r})^{\top}dW_{r}+\frac{v}{2k^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\bigg]

(2.144)

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\zeta|^{k^{\prime}}\right]\right\}^{\frac{v}{k^{\prime}}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\Big\{-\frac{v}{k^{\prime}-v}\int_{0}^{T}(G_{r})^{\top}dW_{r}+\frac{v}{2(k^{\prime}-v)}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\right]\right\}^{\frac{k^{\prime}-v}{k^{\prime}}}.

Applying Hölder’s inequality to the second term on the right-hand side of (
2.144
), we have for
\theta>1
,

(2.145)

\displaystyle\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\Big\{-\frac{v}{k^{\prime}-v}\int_{0}^{T}(G_{r})^{\top}dW_{r}+\frac{v}{2(k^{\prime}-v)}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\right]\right\}^{\frac{k^{\prime}-v}{k^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\Big\{-\frac{\theta v}{k^{\prime}-v}\int_{0}^{T}(G_{r})^{\top}dW_{r}-\frac{\theta^{2}{v}^{2}}{2(k^{\prime}-v)^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\right]\right\}^{\frac{k^{\prime}-v}{\theta k^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\cdot\left\{E\left[\exp\Big\{\Big(\frac{\theta^{2}{v}^{2}}{2(k^{\prime}-v)^{2}(\theta-1)}+\frac{\theta v}{2(k^{\prime}-v)(\theta-1)}\Big)\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\right]\right\}^{\frac{(\theta-1)(k^{\prime}-v)}{\theta k^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\Big\{\Big(\frac{\theta^{2}{v}^{2}}{2(k^{\prime}-v)^{2}(\theta-1)}+\frac{\theta v}{2(k^{\prime}-v)(\theta-1)}\Big)\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\right]\right\}^{\frac{(\theta-1)(k^{\prime}-v)}{\theta k^{\prime}}}.

Letting
\theta=1+\sqrt{\frac{k^{\prime}}{v}}
in (
2.145
), by (
2.143
) and (
2.101
) we have that

(2.146)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\Big\{-\frac{v}{k^{\prime}-v}\int_{0}^{T}(G_{r})^{\top}dW_{r}+\frac{v}{2(k^{\prime}-v)}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{v}}{\sqrt{k^{\prime}}-\sqrt{v}}\right)^{2}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\bigg]\right\}^{\frac{\sqrt{k^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{v}+\sqrt{k^{\prime}}}}\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\Big\{\gamma\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\right]\right\}^{\frac{\sqrt{k^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{v}+\sqrt{k^{\prime}}}}

is finite. Then, combining (
2.144
) and (
2.146
), we have
\mathbb{E}[|\zeta|^{v}]<\infty
,
which implies
\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,k^{\prime}}(0,T)\subset\mathfrak{H}_{p,v}(0,T)
.

Now we prove the uniqueness of the solution
(Y,Z)
in the space
\mathfrak{H}_{p,v}(0,T)
. Let
(Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime})
be another solution to Eq. (
2.98
) in
\mathfrak{H}_{p,v}(0,T)
. We will show that
(Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime})\in\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,v^{\prime}}(0,T)
for some
v^{\prime}\in(1,v)
, which implies that
(Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime})
is also a solution to Eq. (
2.141
) in
\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,v^{\prime}}(0,T)
. Then the uniqueness of the solution to Eq. (
2.98
) follows from the uniqueness of the solution to Eq. (
2.141
).

To show
(Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime})\in\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,v^{\prime}}(0,T)
for some
v^{\prime}\in(1,v)
, it suffices to show

\mathfrak{H}_{p,v}(0,T)\subset\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,v^{\prime}}(0,T)
for some
v^{\prime}\in(1,v)
.
By (
2.102
), there exists
v^{\prime}>1
such that

(2.147)

\gamma>\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{v}}{\sqrt{v}-\sqrt{v^{\prime}}}\Big)^{2}.

Then, for
\zeta\in\mathcal{F}_{T}
with
\mathbb{E}[|\zeta|^{v}]<\infty
, by Hölder’s inequality we have

(2.148)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[|\zeta|^{v^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[|\zeta|^{v^{\prime}}\exp\Big\{\int_{0}^{T}(G_{r})^{\top}dW_{r}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[|\zeta|^{v}\right]\right\}^{\frac{v^{\prime}}{v}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\Big\{\frac{v}{v-v^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{T}(G_{r})^{\top}dW_{r}-\frac{v}{2(v-v^{\prime})}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\right]\right\}^{\frac{v-v^{\prime}}{v}}.

In addition, by Hölder’s inequality, for
\theta^{\prime}:=1+\sqrt{\frac{v^{\prime}}{v}}
,

(2.149)

\displaystyle\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\Big\{\frac{v}{v-v^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{T}(G_{r})^{\top}dW_{r}-\frac{v}{2(v-v^{\prime})}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\right]\right\}^{\frac{v-v^{\prime}}{v}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\frac{\theta^{\prime}v}{v-v^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{T}(G_{r})^{\top}dW_{r}-\frac{{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}v^{2}}{2(v-v^{\prime})^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\bigg]\right\}^{\frac{v-v^{\prime}}{\theta^{\prime}v}}

\displaystyle\cdot\left\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\Big(\frac{{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}v^{2}}{2(\theta^{\prime}-1)(v-v^{\prime})^{2}}-\frac{\theta^{\prime}v}{2(\theta^{\prime}-1)(v-v^{\prime})}\Big)\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\bigg]\right\}^{\frac{(\theta^{\prime}-1)(v-v^{\prime})}{\theta^{\prime}v}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{v}}{\sqrt{v}-\sqrt{v^{\prime}}}\Big)^{2}\int_{0}^{T}|G_{r}|^{2}dr\Big\}\bigg]\right\}^{\frac{(\theta^{\prime}-1)(v-v^{\prime})}{\theta^{\prime}v}}.

Combining (
2.101
), (
2.147
), (
2.148
), and (
2.149
), we have
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[|\zeta|^{v^{\prime}}]<\infty
,
and hence
\mathfrak{H}_{p,v}(0,T)\subset\tilde{\mathfrak{H}}_{p,v^{\prime}}(0,T)
.
∎

Remark 2.9
.

In
(3)
of Assumption
(A2)
, a stronger condition is imposed for
N>1
compared to
N=1
. This is because the solution
\Gamma^{t}_{\cdot}
of Eq. (
2.103
) admits an explicit formula in the one dimension case.
More precisely, denoting
dx^{i}_{r}:=\eta_{i}(dr,X_{r})
, we have

\Gamma^{t}_{s}=I_{N}+\sum_{i=1}^{M}\int_{t}^{s}(\alpha^{i}_{r})^{\top}\Gamma^{t}_{r}dx^{i}_{r}\quad\text{for }s\in[t,T].

When
N=1
, Itô’s formula established in
[
48
, Proposition 2.2]
yields
\Gamma^{t}_{s}=\exp\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{M}\int_{t}^{s}\alpha^{i}_{r}dx^{i}_{r}\right\}
. Thus, the estimate for
\Gamma^{t}_{s}
can be derived from the estimate for
\int_{t}^{s}\alpha^{i}_{r}dx^{i}_{r},
which is bounded by
C\|\alpha\|_{p\text{-}\mathrm{var}}\|x\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-}\mathrm{var}}
for a generic constant
C
, in contrast to the bound in the multi-dimensional case
\log|\Gamma^{t}_{s}|\lesssim(\|\alpha\|_{p\text{-}\mathrm{var}}\|x\|_{\frac{1}{\tau}\text{-}\mathrm{var}})^{\frac{1}{\tau}}
(see (
2.117
)).

Remark 2.10
.

A typical example of
X
satisfying (
2.99
) is the Brownian motion, in which case
\chi=2
. In addition, a function
\eta
with
\|\eta\|_{\tau,\lambda;\beta}<\infty
can be taken as a realization of a fractional Brownian sheet (see
[
48
, Lemma A.4]
).

Remark 2.11

(Comparison with the result in
[
32
]
)
.

When
\eta
is a realization of
(1+d)
-dimensional fractional Brownian sheet
B(t,x)
with Hurst parameters
(H_{0},H,...,H)
, our requirement of the well-posedness for BSDE (
2.98
) is weaker than that in
[
32
]
. More precisely, the result in
[
32
]
only applies to the case
N=1
and
dH<1
(see the condition (2.4) therein), while we allow
N>1
, and moreover,
dH<2
is sufficient for our requirement
\lambda+\beta<2
(for the case
X=W
,
N=1
, and
\chi=2
) in
(A2)
(see
[
48
, Lemma A.4]
).
This is due to the fact that we only consider a realization of B(t,x) without taking the randomization, which ignores the integrability with respect to the fractional Brownian sheet.

Now we provide a probabilistic representation for the solution of the following Young PDE:

(2.150)

\partial_{t}u+Lu+u\partial_{t}\eta=0,\quad u(T,x)=u_{T}(x),

where
Lv(t,x):=\frac{1}{2}\text{tr}[\sigma\sigma^{\top}(x)\nabla^{2}v(t,x)]+b^{\top}(x)\nabla v(t,x)
.
Here, we assume strict uniform ellipticity of
\sigma
, i.e., there exists a positive constant
\nu
such that

(2.151)

(\sigma\sigma^{\top}(x))_{i,j}a_{i}a_{j}\geq\nu|a|^{2},\ \text{ for all }a\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{ and }x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Proposition 2.4
.

Assume
N=1
and
\chi=2
. Suppose that
X^{t,x}
is given by (
2.79
), with
b\in C^{1}_{b}
,
\sigma\in C^{3}_{b}
, and
\sigma\sigma^{\top}
satisfying the strict uniform ellipticity condition (
2.151
). Assume
u_{T}(\cdot)\in C^{\mathrm{Lip}}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
. Assume further that
(\tau,\lambda,\beta)\in(\frac{1}{2},1]\times(0,1]\times[0,\infty)
satisfies
\beta+\lambda<2
and
\lambda+4\tau>4.
Let
(Y^{t,x},Z^{t,x})
be the unique solution to BSDE (
2.98
) with
\alpha\equiv 1
,
f=G=0
,
\xi=u_{T}(X^{t,x}_{T})
, and
X=X^{t,x}
.
Define the function
u
by

(2.152)

u(t,x):=Y^{t,x}_{t}.

Then, for any
\tau^{\prime}\in(0,\frac{\lambda}{2}+\tau-1)
,
u(\cdot,x)\in C^{\tau^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}([0,T];\mathbb{R})
. Moreover,
u

is a weak solution to (
2.150
) in the following sense:

(2.153)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u(t,x)\varphi(x)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{T}(x)\varphi(x)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{t}^{T}u(s,x)L^{*}\varphi(x)dsdx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{t}^{T}u(s,x)\varphi(x)\eta(ds,x)dx,\quad t\in[0,T],

where
\varphi
is any smooth function with compact support,
L^{*}
is the adjoint operator of
L
, and the inner integral in the last term is a Young integral. Further more, we have

(2.154)

u(s,X^{t,x}_{s})=Y^{t,x}_{s},\quad(s,x)\in[t,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Proof.

Note that
(A2)
holds for every
p\in(2,\frac{\lambda}{1-\tau}),

\rho>0
,
q>\frac{2}{2-(\lambda+\beta)}
, and
k>1
. By Theorem
3
, we have the following representation for
Y^{t,x}
:

(2.155)

Y^{t,x}_{s}=\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[u_{T}(X^{t,x}_{T})\exp\Big\{\int_{s}^{T}\eta(dr,X^{t,x}_{r})\Big\}\right],~t\leq s\leq T.

and hence
u(t,x)=\mathbb{E}\left[u_{T}(X^{t,x}_{T})\exp\left\{\int_{t}^{T}\eta(dr,X^{t,x}_{r})\right\}\right]
.
Then, for any
\tau^{\prime}\in(0,\frac{\lambda}{2}+\tau-1)
, by
[
31
, Theorem 4.15]
, we have
u(\cdot,x)\in C^{\tau^{\prime}\text{-H\"{o}l}}(0,T)
, and that
u(t,x)
is a weak solution to Eq. (
2.150
).

By the Markov property of
X^{t,x}
and the fact
X_{r}^{t,x}=X_{r}^{s,X_{s}^{t,x}}
for
t\leq s\leq r\leq T
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[u_{T}(X^{s,x}_{T})\exp\Big\{\int_{s}^{T}\eta(dr,X^{s,x}_{r})\Big\}\right]\Bigg|_{x=X^{t,x}_{s}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[u_{T}(X^{s,X^{t,x}_{s}}_{T})\exp\Big\{\int_{s}^{T}\eta(dr,X^{s,X^{t,x}_{s}}_{r})\Big\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[u_{T}(X^{t,x}_{T})\exp\Big\{\int_{s}^{T}\eta(dr,X^{t,x}_{r})\Big\}\right].

Combining (
2.152
) and (
2.155
), we have

\displaystyle u(s,X^{t,x}_{s})=\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[u_{T}(X^{t,x}_{T})\exp\Big\{\int_{s}^{T}\eta(dr,X^{t,x}_{r})\Big\}\right]=Y^{t,x}_{s}.

This proves (
2.154
).
∎

Remark 2.12
.

In Proposition
2.4
, we assume
\lambda+\beta<2
and
\tau+\frac{\lambda}{4}>1
to validate the Feynman-Kac formula. In contrast, for the well-posedness of BSDE (
2.98
) (see Theorem
3
), we only require
\lambda+\beta<2
and
\tau+\frac{\lambda}{2}>1
(see Remark
2.8
).
This is because the verification of weak solutions requires the temporal Hölder continuity of
u(t,x)=Y_{t}^{t,x}
in order to make sense of the Young integral in (
2.153
), and this in turn requires more regularity of
\eta
(see
[
31
, Proposition 2.11]
).

3.
Applications

3.1.
Nonlinear Feynman-Kac formula

In this section, we study the following Young PDE on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

(3.1)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle-\partial_{t}u=\mathcal{L}u(t,x)+g_{i}(u)\partial_{t}\eta_{i}(t,x),\quad(t,x)\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\\
\displaystyle u(T,x)=h(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\end{cases}

where
\mathcal{L}u:=\frac{1}{2}\text{tr}\left[\sigma\sigma^{\top}\nabla^{2}u\right]+b^{\top}(x)\nabla u+f(t,x,u,\sigma^{\top}\nabla u)
.
Here,
g\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{M}),\eta\in C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M}),\sigma\in C(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
,
b\in C(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
f\in C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, and
h\in C(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})

via our nonlinear Young BSDE. We also assume strict uniform ellipticity (
2.151
) of
\sigma
.

To address the nonlinear Young driver, we shall study the Young PDE (
3.1
) via approximation. We first consider a Cauchy-Dirichlet problem on a bounded domain with the noise
\eta
smooth in time. Let
\{\eta^{m}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\eta^{m}\in C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M})
be a sequence approximation of
\eta
such that
\eta^{m}
is smooth in time and
\partial_{t}\eta^{m}(t,x)
is continuous in
(t,x)
. Let
\{D_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of bounded open subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with smooth boundaries
\partial D_{n}
. We refer to
[
22
, Appendix C.1]
for the definition of smooth boundary (or
C^{\infty}
-boundary). For some fixed
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
, consider the following PDE on
[0,T]\times\bar{D}_{n}
, with
\bar{D}_{n}
being the closure of
D_{n}
,

(3.2)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle-\partial_{t}u(t,x)=\mathcal{L}u(t,x)+g_{i}(u)\partial_{t}\eta^{m}_{i}(t,x),\ t\in(0,T),\quad x\in D_{n},\\
u(t,x)=h(x),\quad(t,x)\in\{T\}\times\bar{D}_{n}\cup(0,T)\times\partial D_{n}.\end{cases}

Definition 3.1
.

We call
u\in C([0,T]\times\bar{D}_{n};\mathbb{R})
a viscosity solution of (
3.2
) if
u
satisfies

u(t,x)=h(x)\ \text{ for }(t,x)\in\{T\}\times\bar{D}_{n}\text{ or }(0,T)\times\partial D_{n},

and the following conditions hold for every
(t,x)\in(0,T)\times D_{n}:

(i)

a+F(t,x,u(t,x),q,\Lambda)\geq 0\text{ if }(a,q,\Lambda)\in\mathscr{P}^{2,+}u(t,x);

(ii)

a+F(t,x,u(t,x),q,\Lambda)\leq 0\text{ if }(a,q,\Lambda)\in\mathscr{P}^{2,-}u(t,x).

Here,
F:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{S}(d)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is defined by (
\mathcal{S}(d)
is the set of symmetric
d\times d
matrices)

(3.3)

F(t,x,y,q,\Lambda):=\frac{1}{2}\text{tr}\left[\sigma(x)\sigma^{\top}(x)\Lambda\right]+b^{\top}(x)q+f(t,x,y,\sigma^{\top}(x)q)+\sum_{i=1}^{M}g_{i}(y)\partial_{t}\eta^{m}_{i}(t,x),

and
\mathscr{P}^{2,+}u(t,x)
(resp.
\mathscr{P}^{2,-}u(t,x)
) is the set of
(a,q,\Lambda)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{S}(d)
such that the following inequality holds as
(s,x^{\prime})\rightarrow(t,x):

u(s,x^{\prime})-u(t,x)\leq(\text{resp. }\geq)\ a(s-t)+(x^{\prime}-x)^{\top}q+\frac{1}{2}(x^{\prime}-x)^{\top}\Lambda(x^{\prime}-x)+o(|s-t|+|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}).

Proposition 3.1
.

Assuming the same conditions as in Theorem
2
together with (
2.151
), Eq. (
3.2
) has a unique viscosity solution.

Proof.

The uniqueness can be obtained by a proper adaption of the comparison theorem
[
15
, Theorem 8.2]
. First, note that the function
F
defined by Eq. (
3.3
) actually does
not
fully meet the assumption of
[
15
, Theorem 8.2]
(see Eq. (8.4) therein), since
F
is not “proper” noting that both
f(t,x,y,z)
and
g(y)\partial_{t}\eta^{m}(t,x)
may not be monotonic in
y
. However, since
D_{n}
is bounded and so is
\{y=u(t,x),x\in D_{n}\}
due to the continuity of
u
, we have that
F
is uniformly Lipschitz in
y
, and then we can follow the argument in
[
5
, Section 5.3]
to prove the comparison result for our equation. Also note that the condition (BC) in Eq. (8.4) in
[
15
]
can be removed if the viscosity subsolution and supersolution are assumed to be continuous (see also the comparison theorem
[
15
, Theorem 7.9]
for elliptic equations), which is our situation.

Now, we prove the existence. For
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
, let
(Y^{n,m;t,x}_{s},Z^{n,m;t,x}_{s})
be the unique solution to the following BSDEs (see
[
47
]
or
[
50
]
for the well-posedness of BSDEs),

(3.4)

\begin{cases}dY_{s}=-f(s,X^{t,x}_{s},Y_{s},Z_{s})ds-g_{i}(Y_{s})\eta^{m}_{i}(ds,X^{t,x}_{s})-Z_{s}dW_{s},\quad s\in[0,T^{t,x}_{D_{n}}],\\
Y_{T^{t,x}_{D_{n}}}=h(X^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}_{D_{n}}}),\end{cases}

where
X^{t,x}
is given by (
2.79
), and

(3.5)

T^{t,x}_{D_{n}}:=T\land\inf\{s>t;\ X^{t,x}_{s}\notin\bar{D}_{n}\}.

Then, the function

(3.6)

u^{n,m}(t,x):=Y^{n,m;t,x}_{t}

is continuous in
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\bar{D}_{n}
by Lemma
B.3
,
and it is a viscosity solution of (
3.2
) by
[
46
, Theorem 4.6]
, noting that the set of regular points
\Lambda_{n}
defined in (
2.81
) coincides with the closed set
\partial D_{n}
by the condition (
2.151
) and Remark
2.7
.
Therefore,
u=u^{n,m}
is a viscosity solution of (
3.2
).
∎

Now we are ready to define the solution of the Young PDE (
3.1
).

Definition 3.2
.

Assume
\eta\in C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M})
. We call
u\in C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
a solution of Eq. (
3.1
) if there exists a sequence of functions
\{\eta^{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M})
and a sequence of bounded open domains
\{D_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

(i)

\eta^{m}
is smooth in time,
\partial_{t}\eta^{m}(t,x)
is continuous in
(t,x)
, and

\eta^{m}\rightarrow\eta\text{ in }C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M})\text{ as }m\rightarrow\infty;

(ii)

the boundary
\partial D_{n}
of
D_{n}
is smooth, and
D_{n}\uparrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
;

(iii)

for each
n
and
m
, Eq. (
3.2
) admits a unique viscosity solution
u^{n,m},
and for every
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
and every
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
n_{\varepsilon},m_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
|u^{n,m}(t,x)-u(t,x)|\leq\varepsilon
for all
n\geq n_{\varepsilon}
and
m\geq m_{\varepsilon}
.

We call
u
a unique solution of Eq. (
3.1
) if
u
is independent of the choice of
\left\{D_{n}\right\}_{n}
and
\left\{\eta^{m}\right\}_{m}
.

Remark 3.1
.

We explain Definition
3.2
for Young PDEs, which involves two approximations: the approximation of the noise
\eta^{m}\to\eta
and the approximation of the domain
D_{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

The first approximation heuristically says that the solution of the PDE with an irregular driver
\eta(dt,x)
can be defined as the limit of the solutions to the PDEs with smooth drivers
\partial_{t}\eta^{m}(t,x)dt
. This idea was already used, for instance, to define the solution to BSDEs with rough drivers in
[
18
]
.

The second approximation reflects the intuition that
the solution of the PDE on the whole space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
can be obtained by taking a limit of a sequence of solutions to the PDEs on bounded domains. This idea has appeared, for instance, in
[
8
]
where the convergence of solutions on bounded domains to the solution on the whole space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
was investigated for heat equation driven by Gaussian noise which is white in time and correlated in space.

We introduce some notations related to the approximations in Definition (
3.2
). Assume the same conditions as in Theorem
2
, and let
D_{n}
and
\eta^{m}
be as in Definition
3.2
. Denote

u^{\infty,m}(t,x):=Y^{\infty,m;t,x}_{t}\text{ and }u^{n,\infty}(t,x):=Y^{n,\infty;t,x}_{t},

where,
(Y^{\infty,m;t,x},Z^{\infty,m;t,x})\in\bigcap_{q>1}\mathfrak{H}_{p,q}(0,T)
is the unique solution to the following BSDE satisfying the condition (
2.82
) (see Theorem
1
and Theorem
2
),

(3.7)

Y_{s}=h(X^{t,x}_{T})+\int_{s}^{T}f(r,X^{t,x}_{r},Y_{r},Z_{r})dr+\sum_{i=1}^{M}\int_{s}^{T}g_{i}(Y_{r})\eta^{m}_{i}(dr,X^{t,x}_{r})-\int_{s}^{T}Z_{r}dW_{r},\quad s\in[t,T],

and

(Y^{n,\infty;t,x},Z^{n,\infty;t,x})\in\bigcap_{q>1}\mathfrak{B}_{p,q}(0,T)
is the unique solution to BSDE (
3.4
) with
\eta^{m}
replaced by
\eta
(the well-posedness can be seen in
[
48
, Proposition 4.1]
).
In addition, denote by
(Y^{\infty,\infty},Z^{\infty,\infty})
the unique solution to BSDE (
3.4
) where
\eta^{m}
and
T^{t,x}{D_{n}}
are replaced by
