\eta
and
T
, respectively, and which belongs to
\bigcap_{q>1}\mathfrak{H}_{p,q}(0,T)
and satisfies (
2.82
). Denote

u^{\infty,\infty}(t,x):=Y^{\infty,\infty;t,x}_{t}.

We remark that similar to
Y^{n,m;t,x}_{t}
,
Y^{n,\infty;t,x}_{t}
is a.s. a constant, and by Corollary
2.1
,
Y^{\infty,m;t,x}_{t}
and
Y^{\infty,\infty;t,x}_{t}
are a.s. constants. Hence, functions
u^{n,\infty},

u^{\infty,m},
and
u^{\infty,\infty}
are well defined.

We also introduce the following space for the Young driver
\eta
: for
(\tau,\lambda,\beta)\in(0,1]\times(0,1]\times[0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}_{0}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M})

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\eta\in C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M}):\text{ there exists }\{\eta^{m}\}_{m\geq 1}\subset C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}\text{ such that }

\displaystyle\eta^{m}\text{ is smooth in time, }\partial_{t}\eta^{m}(t,x)\text{ is continuous, and }\lim_{m}\|\eta^{m}-\eta\|_{\tau,\lambda;\beta}=0\Big\}.

Clearly,
C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}_{0}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M})
is a subset of
C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M})
. Moreover, for every
\hat{\tau}\in(0,\tau)
, we have
C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M})\subset C^{\hat{\tau},\lambda;\beta}_{0}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M})
by approximation result proved in
[
48
, Lemma A.3]
.

Now, we are ready to prove the existence and uniqueness of the solution to the Young PDE (
3.1
).

Proposition 3.2
.

Assume the same conditions as in Theorem
2
. In addition, assume that
f
is jointly continuous in
(t,x,y,z)
,
\sigma
satisfies condition (
2.151
), and
\eta\in C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}_{0}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{M})
. Then,
u:=u^{\infty,\infty}
is the unique solution of the Young PDE (
3.1
) in the sense of Definition
3.2
. Furthermore, the solution map
S:C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}_{0}\to C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
defined by
S(\eta)=u
is continuous, where the topology of
C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}_{0}
is induced by the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\tau,\lambda;\beta}
and
C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
is equipped with the locally uniform convergence topology.

Proof.

Recall that, from the proof Proposition
3.1
,
u^{n,m}(t,x):=Y^{n,m;t,x}_{t}
given by Eq. (
3.6
) is a unique viscosity solution of Eq. (
3.2
).
In order to prove
u^{\infty,\infty}
solves (
3.1
) in the sense of Definition
3.2
, it suffices to show that
u^{n,m}(t,x)\to u^{\infty,\infty}(t,x)
as
n,m\rightarrow\infty
.

We aim to establish an estimate on
|u^{n,m}(t,x)-u^{\infty,m}(t,x)|
which is uniform in
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K
, where
K
is a compact subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. To this end, we shall apply Proposition
2.2
, and we need to verify that for all
m\geq 1
and
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K
, the family of equations (
3.7
) share a common parameter
{\Theta_{2}}
, where
{\Theta_{2}}
is defined in the beginning of Section
2
. Firstly, the terminal time
T
and the function
(f,g)
in BSDE (
3.4
) do not depend on
m
or
(t,x)
. Secondly, since
\sup_{m\geq 1}\|\eta^{m}\|_{\tau,\lambda;\beta}<\infty
,
BSDEs (
3.4
) share the same parameter
(\tau,\lambda,\beta,p,k,\varepsilon)
in Assumption
(A1)
. Finally, according to Example
2.1
,
\Xi_{\cdot}:=h(X^{t,x}_{\cdot})
satisfies Assumption
(T)
with
k=2
and the same constant
C_{1}
as in (
2.4
) for all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K
.

To apply Proposition
2.2
, we still need to show that
there exists a sequence of numbers
\{p_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
with
p_{n}\downarrow 0
such that

\mathbb{P}\left\{T^{t,x}_{D_{n}}\neq T\right\}\leq p_{n},\text{ for all }n\geq 1\text{ and }(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K,

where
T_{D_{n}}^{t,x}
is defined in (
3.5
). Indeed, by the proof of Lemma
2.2
,
there exists positive constants
C
and
C_{K}=C(K)
such that, for
n
sufficiently large satisfying
K\subset\left\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d};\ |y|\leq\inf_{z\in\partial D_{n}}|z|\right\}
,

\sup_{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K}\mathbb{P}\{T^{t,x}_{D_{n}}\neq T\}\leq\sup_{x\in K}\mathbb{P}\{T^{0,x}_{D_{n}}\neq T\}\leq C_{K}\exp\{-C\inf_{y\in\partial D_{n}}|y|^{2}\}=:p_{n},

where the second inequality follows from the same argument used in the proof of Lemma
2.2
which leads to (
2.7
).

Now we can apply Proposition
2.2
to (
3.7
) and get that, for every
\delta>0
, there exists
n^{\prime}\geq 1
, independent of
m\geq 1
and
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K
, such that for all
n\geq n^{\prime}
,

\left|Y^{n,m;t,x}_{t}-Y^{\infty,m;t,x}_{t}\right|+\left|Y^{n,\infty;t,x}_{t}-Y^{\infty,\infty;t,x}_{t}\right|\leq\frac{\delta}{4}.

Hence, for every
\delta>0
, there exists an
n^{\prime}\geq 1
such that, for all
n\geq n^{\prime}
and
m\geq 1
,

(3.8)

|u^{n,m}(t,x)-u^{n^{\prime},m}(t,x)|+|u^{n^{\prime},\infty}(t,x)-u^{\infty,\infty}(t,x)|\leq\frac{\delta}{2},\ \forall(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K.

By the stability of solutions to BSDEs with different drivers, which is established in
[
48
, Corollary 3.1]
, for every fixed
n\geq 1
, we have
\|Y^{n,m;t,x}_{t}-Y^{n,\infty;t,x}_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\rightarrow 0
uniformly in
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K
, as
m\rightarrow\infty
.
Hence, we have

u^{n,m}(t,x)\rightarrow u^{n,\infty}(t,x)
uniformly in
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K
as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Then, there exists
m^{\prime}(n^{\prime})\geq 1
such that

|u^{n^{\prime},m}(t,x)-u^{n^{\prime},\infty}(t,x)|\leq\frac{\delta}{2}
for all
m\geq m^{\prime}(n^{\prime})
.
Hence, by (
3.8
), we have for all
n\geq n^{\prime}
and
m\geq m^{\prime}(n^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle|u^{n,m}(t,x)-u^{\infty,\infty}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\leq|u^{n,m}(t,x)-u^{n^{\prime},m}(t,x)|+|u^{n^{\prime},m}(t,x)-u^{n^{\prime},\infty}(t,x)|

\displaystyle+|u^{n^{\prime},\infty}(t,x)-u^{\infty,\infty}(t,x)|\leq\delta.

This implies the uniform convergence
u^{n,m}\to u^{\infty,\infty}(t,x)
on
[0,T]\times K
, as
n,m\to\infty
. Noting the continuity of
u^{n,m},
we also have
u^{\infty,\infty}\in C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Furthermore, since
\{\eta^{m}\}_{m}
and
\{D_{n}\}_{n}
are arbitrarily chosen,
u^{\infty,\infty}
is the unique solution in the sense of Definition
3.2
.

Finally, we prove the continuity of the solution map. Let
\{\eta^{(j)}\}_{j\geq 1}
be a sequence of functions converging to
\eta
in
C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}_{0}([0,T]\times;\mathbb{R}^{M})
, and
denote by
u^{\infty,(j)}
the solution to Eq. (
3.1
) with
\eta
replaced by
\eta^{(j)}
. Similarly, let
(Y^{n,(j);t,x},Z^{n,(j);t,x})
be the unique solution to Eq. (
3.4
) with
\eta^{m}
replaced by
\eta^{(j)}
, and denote
u^{n,(j)}(t,x):=Y^{n,(j);t,x}_{t}
for
n\geq 1
. By the same argument leading to the inequality (
3.8
), for every
\delta>0
, there exists an
n^{\prime}\geq 1
independent of
j
such that, for all
j\geq 1
,

|u^{n^{\prime},(j)}(t,x)-u^{\infty,(j)}(t,x)|+|u^{n^{\prime},\infty}(t,x)-u^{\infty,\infty}(t,x)|\leq\frac{\delta}{2},\ \forall(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K.

In addition, by
[
48
, Corollary 3.1]
again, there exists
j^{\prime}(n^{\prime})
such that for all
j\geq j^{\prime}(n^{\prime})
,

|u^{n^{\prime},(j)}(t,x)-u^{n^{\prime},\infty}(t,x)|\leq\frac{\delta}{2},\ \forall(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K.

Hence, for all
j\geq j^{\prime}(n^{\prime})
, we have

|u^{\infty,(j)}(t,x)-u^{\infty,\infty}(t,x)|\leq\delta,\ \forall(t,x)\in[0,T]\times K,

and this yields the continuity of the solution map.
∎

The following PDE driven by fractional Brownian sheet (which is thus an SPDE)

(3.9)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle-\partial_{t}u(t,x)=\mathcal{L}u(t,x)+g(u)\partial_{t}B(t,x),(t,x)\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\\[10.0pt]
\displaystyle u(T,x)=h(x),\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\end{cases}

can be solved pathwisely as a Young PDE (
3.1
), provided that the sample paths of
B(t,x)
lives in some
C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Indeed, by
[
48
, Lemma A.4]
, the paths of
B(t,x)
belong to
C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
a.s., where
(\tau,\lambda,\beta)
essentially satisfies

\tau<H_{0},

\lambda<\min_{1\leq j\leq d}H_{j},
and
\beta>-\lambda+\sum_{j=1}^{d}H_{j}
. If we assume further
H_{j}\equiv H
for
j=1,2,\dots,d
for simplicity, then, to ensure the existence of
\varepsilon
and
p
such that
(\tau,\lambda,\beta,\varepsilon,p)
satisfies Assumption
(A1)
, it suffices to assume

(3.10)

H_{0}+\frac{H}{2}>1\ \text{ and }dH<2H_{0}-1,

as explained in Remark
2.2
. The shaded regions of
(H,H_{0})
satisfying (
3.10
) are demonstrated in Figure
2
and Figure
2
for
1
-dimensional and
d
-dimensional respectively.

Figure 1.
1
-dim region of
(H,H_{0})

Figure 2.
d
-dim region of
(H,H_{0})

Remark 3.2
.

In the literature of SPDE, if an SPDE is solved pathwisely via deterministic PDEs, it is usually called a
Stratonovich
equation. In contrast, in the analysis of the so-called
Itô-Skorohod
equation, probabilistic instruments such as Itô calculus and Malliavin calculus play a critical role. For related results, we refer to
[
34
,
33
]
, where the Feynman-Kac formulae for linear heat equations driven by fractional Brownian sheets were investigated.

3.2.
Localization error estimate for non-Lipschitz PDEs

In this section, we study the following BSDE with a non-Lipschitz coefficient
F:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}^{N\times d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{N}
:

(3.11)

Y_{t}=h(X_{T})+\int_{t}^{T}F(r,X_{r},Y_{r},Z_{r})dr-\int_{t}^{T}Z_{r}dW_{r},\quad t\in[0,T].

Remark 3.3
.

Consider
F(t,x,y,z)=v(x)g(y)
, where
g\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{N};\mathbb{R}^{N})
and
v:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfies

|v(x_{1})-v(x_{2})|\lesssim(1+|x_{1}|+|x_{2}|)^{\beta}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\lambda},\ \text{for all }x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

for some
(\lambda,\beta)\in(0,1]\times[0,\infty)
with
\lambda+\beta<1
.
The above equation can be viewed as a BSDE driven by a nonlinear Young integral. More precisely, suppose
X
satisfies
(A0)
and
h\in C^{\text{Lip}}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{N})
.
Then, the well-posedness of Eq. (
3.11
) follows directly from Theorem
1
and Theorem
2
, noting that
\eta(t,x):=v(x)t
belongs to
C^{1,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

In the following, we will apply a new localization method to solve stochastic Lipschitz BSDEs. This method will be applied to estimate localization errors for PDEs with non-Lipschitz coefficients (see Proposition
3.4
).

Proposition 3.3
.

Assume
X
satisfies Assumption
(A0)
. Let
0\leq\theta_{1}<1
,
0\leq\theta_{2}<2
,
\theta_{3}\geq 0
,
\mu\in(0,1]
, and
\mu^{\prime}\geq 0
. Assume
F=f_{0}+F_{0}
and for all
x,x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},z,z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times d},y,y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{N},
and
t\in[0,T]
, the functions
h
,
f_{0}
, and
F_{0}
satisfy

(3.12)

\begin{cases}h\in C^{\mathrm{Lip}}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{N});\ |f_{0}(t,x,0,0)|\leq C\left(1+|x|^{\theta_{3}}\right);\\
|f_{0}(t,x,y_{1},z_{1})-f_{0}(t,x,y_{2},z_{2})|\leq C\left(|y_{1}-y_{2}|+|z_{1}-z_{2}|\right);\\
|F_{0}(t,x,y,z)|\leq C\left(1+|x|^{\theta_{3}}\right);\\
|F_{0}(t,x,y_{1},z)-F_{0}(t,x,y_{2},z)|\leq C(1+|x|^{\theta_{2}})|y_{1}-y_{2}|;\\
|F_{0}(t,x,y,z_{1})-F_{0}(t,x,y,z_{2})|\leq C(1+|x|^{\theta_{1}})|z_{1}-z_{2}|;\\
|F(t,x_{1},y,z)-F(t,x_{2},y,z)|\leq C|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\mu}(1+|x_{1}|^{\mu^{\prime}}+|x_{2}|^{\mu^{\prime}});\end{cases}

where
C
is a positive constant. Then, BSDE (
3.11
) has a unique solution
(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{H}^{2}(0,T)
,
where

(3.13)

\mathfrak{H}^{q}(0,T):=\left\{(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{B}([0,T]);\ \mathbb{E}\left[\|Y\|^{q}_{\infty;[0,T]}\right]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}|Z_{r}|^{2}dr\Big|^{\frac{q}{2}}\Big]<\infty\right\}

for
q\geq 1
.

Proof.

The proof follows the approach used in the proof of Theorem
1
. We only consider the case when
N=1
(the multi-dimensional case can be proved in the same manner as
Appendix C
The proof of Theorem
for the general case
of Theorem
1
). We also assume
d=1
without loss of generality.

Let
T_{n}
be defined in (
2.6
). Since
F(t,X_{t},y,z)\mathbf{1}_{[0,T_{n}]}(t)
is uniformly Lipschitz in
(y,z)
, the following BSDEs have a unique solution in
\mathfrak{H}^{2}(0,T)
, for
n\geq 1
,

(3.14)

\begin{cases}dY^{n}_{t}=-F(t,X_{t},Y^{n}_{t},Z^{n}_{t})dt+Z^{n}_{t}dW_{t},\ t\in[0,T_{n}],\\[3.0pt]
Y^{n}_{T_{n}}=h(X_{T_{n}}).\end{cases}

For
t\in[0,T]
, denote

\left\{\begin{aligned} a^{n}_{t}&:=\frac{F(t,X_{t},Y^{n+1}_{t},Z^{n+1}_{t})-F(t,X_{t},Y^{n}_{t},Z^{n+1}_{t})}{Y^{n+1}_{t}-Y^{n}_{t}}\mathbf{1}_{\{Y^{n+1}_{t}\neq Y^{n}_{t}\}}(t),\\
b^{n}_{t}&:=\frac{F(t,X_{t},Y^{n}_{t},Z^{n+1}_{t})-F(t,X_{t},Y^{n}_{t},Z^{n}_{t})}{Z^{n+1}_{t}-Z^{n}_{t}}\mathbf{1}_{\{Z^{n+1}_{t}\neq Z^{n}_{t}\}}(t),\end{aligned}\right.

and

\Gamma^{n}_{t}:=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\exp\left\{\int_{t\land T_{n}}^{T_{n}}2\left(a^{n}_{r}-\frac{1}{2}|b^{n}_{r}|^{2}\right)dr+\int_{t\land T_{n}}^{T_{n}}2b^{n}_{r}dW_{r}\right\}\right].

By (
3.12
), it follows that
|b^{n}_{t}|^{2}+|a^{n}_{t}|\lesssim 1+|X_{t}|^{(2\theta_{1})\vee\theta_{2}}
. This estimate, combined with the boundedness of
|X_{\cdot\land T_{n}}|\leq n
when
n\geq|x|
, yields that there exists a constant
C^{\diamond}>0
such that

(3.15)

\esssup_{t\in[0,T],\omega\in\Omega}|\Gamma^{n}_{t}|\lesssim\exp\left\{C^{\diamond}n^{(2\theta_{1})\vee\theta_{2}}\right\},\text{ for all }n\geq|x|.

On the other hand, by the triangular inequality we have

\|Y^{n}_{T_{n}}-Y^{n+1}_{T_{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\|h(X_{T_{n}})-h(X_{T_{n+1}})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|Y^{n+1}_{T_{n}}-h(X_{T_{n+1}})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

For the first term of the right-hand side, we have
\|h(X_{T_{n}})-h(X_{T_{n+1}})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|X_{T_{n}}-X_{T_{n+1}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\left\|T_{n+1}-T_{n}\right\|^{1/2}_{L^{1}(\Omega)}.
For the second one, by
[
48
, Proposition 4.3]
with
S_{0}=T_{n+1},

S_{1}=T_{n},

(Y,Z)=(Y^{n+1},Z^{n+1})
,
\xi=h(X_{T_{n+1}}),

\eta(\cdot,\cdot)\equiv 0,

g(\cdot)\equiv 0,

p=3,

\tau=1,

\lambda=\frac{1}{2},

\beta=0,

\varepsilon=\frac{1}{2(\theta_{3}\vee 1)},

f(t,y,z)=f_{0}(t,X_{t},y,z)+F_{0}(t,X_{t},Y^{n+1}_{t},Z^{n+1}_{t})
, and
q=2
, and by the inequality
\|h(X_{T_{n+1}})\|^{2}_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lesssim 1+|x|^{2}
for all
n\geq|x|,
we have

\left\|Y^{n+1}_{T_{n}}-h(X_{T_{n+1}})\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim(1+|x|^{2\theta_{3}\vee 2})\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[|T_{n+1}-T_{n}|^{8}\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}[h(X_{T_{n+1}})]\|^{2}_{3\text{-var};[T_{n},T_{n+1}]}\right].

In addition, by the BDG inequality for
p
-variation, Doob’s maximal inequality, and the same calculation as in Example
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[h(X_{T_{n+1}})\right]\right\|^{2}_{3\text{-var};[T_{n},T_{n+1}]}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left|h(X_{T_{n+1}})-h(X_{T})\right|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\left[h(X_{T})\right]\right\|^{2}_{3\text{-var};[T_{n},T]}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\{\mathbb{E}\left[|T-T_{n+1}|\right]\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In addition, by Lemma
2.2
, there exists a constant
C^{\star}
such that
\left\|T-T_{n}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\lesssim\exp\left\{-C^{\star}(n-|x|)^{2}\right\}.
Hence, there exists a constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

(3.16)

\|Y^{n}_{T_{n}}-Y^{n+1}_{T_{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim(1+|x|^{\theta_{3}\vee 1})\exp\left\{-C^{\prime}(n-|x|)^{2}\right\},\text{ for all }n\geq|x|.

By (
3.15
), (
3.16
), and the same procedure leading to (
2.53
), we have for all
n\geq|x|,

(3.17)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y^{n}-Y^{n+1}\|_{\infty;[0,T_{n}]}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|Y^{n}_{T_{n}}-Y^{n+1}_{T_{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\bigg\{\esssup_{t\in[0,T],\omega\in\Omega}|\Gamma^{n}_{t}|\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+|x|^{\theta_{3}\vee 1})\exp\left\{C^{\diamond}n^{(2\theta_{1})\vee\theta_{2}}-\frac{C^{\prime}}{2}n^{2}+2C^{\prime}|x|^{2}\right\}.

In view of the (
3.17
) and the fact that
(2\theta_{1})\vee\theta_{2}<2
, there exists a continuous adapted process
Y
on
[0,T]
such that

(3.18)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y^{n}-Y\|_{\infty;[0,T_{n}]}\right]=0.

Thus, by the same argument leading to (
2.56
), there exists a process
Z
such that
(Y,Z)
solves Eq. (
3.11
) and
(Y,Z)\in\mathfrak{H}^{q}(0,T)
for every
q>1
.

The uniqueness can be proved in a similar way to the existence. Assume the pair
(\tilde{Y},\tilde{Z})\in\mathfrak{H}^{2}(0,T)
is a solution of (
3.11
). Let

\left\{\begin{aligned} \tilde{a}^{n}_{t}&:=\frac{F(t,X_{t},\tilde{Y}_{t},\tilde{Z}_{t})-F(t,X_{t},Y^{n}_{t},\tilde{Z}_{t})}{\tilde{Y}_{t}-Y^{n}_{t}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tilde{Y}_{t}\neq Y^{n}_{t}\}}(t),\\
\tilde{b}^{n}_{t}&:=\frac{F(t,X_{t},Y^{n}_{t},\tilde{Z}_{t})-F(t,X_{t},Y^{n}_{t},Z^{n}_{t})}{\tilde{Z}_{t}-Z^{n}_{t}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tilde{Z}_{t}\neq Z^{n}_{t}\}}(t),\end{aligned}\right.

and

\tilde{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}:=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\exp\left\{\int_{t\land T_{n}}^{T_{n}}2\left(\tilde{a}^{n}_{r}-\frac{1}{2}|\tilde{b}^{n}_{r}|^{2}\right)dr+\int_{t\land T_{n}}^{T_{n}}2\tilde{b}^{n}_{r}dW_{r}\right\}\right].
From (
3.12
) it follows that

\log\Big(\esssup_{s\in[0,T],\omega\in\Omega}|\tilde{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}|\Big)\lesssim 1+n^{(2\theta_{1})\vee\theta_{2}}.

Note that
(\tilde{Y},\tilde{Z})
solves the following BSDE,

\tilde{Y}_{t}=h(X_{T})+\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{F}(r,\tilde{Y}_{r},\tilde{Z}_{r})dr-\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{Z}_{r}dW_{r},\quad t\in[0,T],

where
\tilde{F}(t,y,z):=f_{0}(t,X_{t},y,z)+F_{0}(t,X_{t},\tilde{Y}_{t},\tilde{Z}_{t})
is uniformly Lipschitz in
(y,z)
. Also note that
\|\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{F}(t,0,0)|^{2}dt\|_{L^{q/2}(\Omega)}<\infty
for every
q>1.
Then, by El Karoui et al.
[
21
, Theorem 5.1]
,
(\tilde{Y},\tilde{Z})\in\mathfrak{H}^{q}(0,T)
for every
q>1.
Thus, since
\tilde{Y}
is a semimartingale, by the BDG inequality for
p
-variation,
(\tilde{Y},\tilde{Z})\in\mathfrak{H}_{3,q}(0,T)
for every
q>1.
Therefore, by
[
48
, Corollary 4.2]
with
S=T_{n}
,
(Y,Z)=(\tilde{Y},\tilde{Z}),

\eta(\cdot,\cdot)\equiv 0,

g(\cdot)\equiv 0,

p=3,

\tau=1,

\lambda=\frac{1}{2},

\beta=0,

\varepsilon=\frac{1}{2(\theta_{3}\vee 1)},

f(t,y,z)=\tilde{F}(t,y,z),
and
q=2
, we have

\|\tilde{Y}_{T_{n}}-h(X_{T})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\tilde{Y}\|^{2}_{\infty;[T_{n},T]}\right]\lesssim_{x}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[|T-T_{n}|^{8}\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}[h(X_{T})]\|^{2}_{3\text{-var};[T_{n},T]}\right].

Then, by repeating the procedure that leads to (
3.18
), we have
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y^{n}-\tilde{Y}\|_{\infty;[0,T_{n}]}\right]=0
.
Recall that
(Y,Z)
is the solution satisfying (
3.18
). Then we have
\tilde{Y}=Y
. Furthermore, noting

\int_{0}^{t}\left(F(r,X_{r},\tilde{Y}_{r},\tilde{Z}_{r})-F(r,X_{r},Y_{r},Z_{r})\right)dr=\int_{0}^{t}\left(\tilde{Z}_{r}-Z_{r}\right)dW_{r},

it follows that
\tilde{Z}=Z
.
∎

We now study the localization error for the following PDE,

(3.19)

\begin{cases}-\partial_{t}u(t,x)=Lu(t,x)+F(t,x,u(t,x),(\sigma^{\top}\nabla u)(t,x)),\ t\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\\[3.0pt]
u(T,x)=h(x),\end{cases}

where
Lu(t,x):=\frac{1}{2}\text{tr}\left\{\sigma(x)\sigma^{\top}(x)\nabla^{2}u(t,x)\right\}+b^{\top}(x)\nabla u(t,x)
.
According to Li et al.
[
39
, Theorem 3.17]
,
u(t,x):=Y^{t,x}_{t}
, where
Y^{t,x}
is the unique solution of Eq. (
3.11
) on
[t,T]
with
X
replaced by
X^{t,x}
(
X^{t,x}
is given by (
2.79
)), is a viscosity solution to (
3.19
).

Let

D_{n}:=\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d};|x|\leq n\right\}
and assume strict uniform ellipticity (
2.151
) for
\sigma
. Note that
D_{n}
is compact. By Proposition
3.1
, the following Dirichlet problem admits a unique viscosity solution in
C([0,T]\times\bar{D}_{n}),

(3.20)

\begin{cases}-\partial_{t}u^{n}(t,x)=Lu^{n}(t,x)+F(t,x,u^{n}(t,x),(\sigma^{\top}\nabla u^{n})(t,x)),\ (t,x)\in(0,T)\times D_{n},\\[3.0pt]
u^{n}(t,x)=h(x),\ (t,x)\in\{T\}\times\bar{D}_{n}\cup(0,T)\times\partial D_{n},\end{cases}

and
u^{n}(t,x)=Y^{n;t,x}_{t},
where
(Y^{n;t,x},Z^{n;t,x})
is the unique solution of Eq. (
3.14
) on
[t,T]
with
X
replaced by
X^{t,x},
and
T_{n}
replaced by
T^{t,x}_{n}:=T\land\inf\left\{r>t;X^{t,x}_{r}\notin\bar{D}_{n}\right\}
. Then
u^{n}
converges to
u
as
n\to\infty
, and the convergence rate is provided in the proposition below.

Proposition 3.4
.

Assume that
(F,h)
satisfies the assumptions of Proposition
3.3
,
F
is jointly continuous in
(t,x,y,z)
,
(b,\sigma)
is independent of
t
and satisfies Assumption
(A0)
. Assume further that (
2.151
) holds. Let
u(t,x):=Y^{t,x}_{t}
(resp.
u^{n}
) be the viscosity solution of Eq. (
3.19
) (resp. Eq. (
3.20
)) mentioned above. Then there exist constants
C^{\star},C^{\prime}>0
such that for all
n\geq|x|,

|u^{n}(t,x)-u(t,x)|\leq C^{\star}\exp\left\{-n^{2}/C^{\star}+C^{\star}|x|^{2}\right\},\text{ and }\ |u(t,x)|\leq C^{\prime}(1+|x|^{\theta_{3}\vee 1}).

Proof.

By (
3.17
), there exist constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that for all
n\geq|x|,

|Y^{n;t,x}_{t}-Y^{t,x}_{t}|\leq\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}|Y^{m;t,x}_{t}-Y^{m+1;t,x}_{t}|\lesssim(1+|x|^{\theta_{3}\vee 1})\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}\exp\left\{-C_{1}m^{2}+C_{2}|x|^{2}\right\}.

Thus,
|u^{n}(t,x)-u(t,x)|=|Y^{n;t,x}_{t}-Y^{t,x}_{t}|\lesssim(1+|x|^{\theta_{3}\vee 1})\exp\left\{-C_{3}n^{2}+C_{2}|x|^{2}\right\}
for some
C_{3}>0
. This proves the first estimate.

By
[
48
, Corollary 4.1]
with
X=X^{t,x},

(Y,Z)=(Y^{t,x},Z^{t,x}),

\eta(\cdot,\cdot)\equiv 0,

g(\cdot)\equiv 0,

p=3,

\tau=1,

\lambda=\frac{1}{2},

\beta=0,

\varepsilon=\frac{1}{2(\theta_{3}\vee 1)},

f(s,y,z)=f_{0}(s,X^{t,x}_{s},y,z)+F_{0}(s,X^{t,x}_{s},Y^{t,x}_{s},Z^{t,x}_{s}),
and
q=2
, it follows that
|u(t,x)|=|Y^{t,x}_{t}|\lesssim 1+|x|^{\theta_{3}\vee 1}
, which proves the second estimate. ∎

Appendix A
Some facts on processes with space-time regularity

The tower rule presented the following two lemmas are used in
2.3.
Linear BSDEs
of Theorem
3
.

Lemma A.1
.

Suppose Assumption
(H0)
holds. Assume
\eta\in C^{\tau,\lambda}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Let
A:\Omega\times[t,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a continuous process with finite p-variation a.s., such that
\mathbb{E}\left[|A_{r}|\right]<\infty
for every
r\in[t,T]
.
Assume that the process
(\omega,r)\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{r}\left[A_{r}\right](\omega)
has a continuous modification that has finite p-variation a.s. Let
X:\Omega\times[t,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
B:\Omega\times[t,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be two continuous adapted processes with finite p-variation a.s. Assume

(A.1)

\mathbb{E}\left[\|AB\|_{\infty;[t,T]}\left(1+\|X\|^{\lambda}_{p\text{-}\mathrm{var};[t,T]}\right)+\|AB\|_{p\text{-}\mathrm{var};[t,T]}\right]<\infty.

In addition, let
C_{r}=\mathbb{E}_{r}[A_{r}]B_{r}
and assume further

(A.2)

\mathbb{E}\left[\|C\|_{\infty;[t,T]}\left(1+\|X\|^{\lambda}_{p\text{-}\mathrm{var};[t,T]}\right)+\|C\|_{p\text{-}\mathrm{var};[t,T]}\right]<\infty.

Then, we have

(A.3)

\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T}A_{r}B_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\Big\|_{p\text{-}\mathrm{var};[t,T]}\right]<\infty.

Moreover, the following tower rule holds,

(A.4)

\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}A_{r}B_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{r}\left[A_{r}\right]B_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\right].

Proof.

For
[u,v]\subset[t,T],
by
[
48
, Proposition 2.1]
with
y_{r}=A_{r}B_{r}
and
x_{r}=X_{r},
we have

\displaystyle\left|\int^{v}_{u}A_{r}B_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})-A_{u}B_{u}\big(\eta(v,X_{u})-\eta(u,X_{u})\big)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\tau,\lambda,p}\|\eta\|_{\tau,\lambda}|u-v|^{\tau}\left(\|AB\|_{\infty;[u,v]}\|X\|^{\lambda}_{p\text{-var};[u,v]}+\|AB\|_{p\text{-var};[u,v]}\right).

Then, for a partition
\pi
on
[t,T]
, by the super-additivity of the right-hand side, we have,

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{t}^{T}A_{r}B_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})-\sum_{[t_{i},t_{i+1}]\in\pi}\big(A_{t_{i}}B_{t_{i}}\eta(t_{i+1},X_{t_{i}})-A_{t_{i}}B_{t_{i}}\eta(t_{i},X_{t_{i}})\big)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\tau,\lambda,p}\|\eta\|_{\tau,\lambda}|T-t|^{\tau}\left(\|AB\|_{\infty;[t,T]}\|X\|^{\lambda}_{p\text{-var};[t,T]}+\|AB\|_{p\text{-var};[t,T]}\right),

the right-hand side of which is integrable by (
A.1
). Moreover,
[
48
, Proposition 2.1]
and (
A.1
) yields

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\int_{\cdot}^{T}A_{r}B_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\right\|_{p\text{-var};[t,T]}\right]<\infty.

Hence, the set of random variables
\left\{\sum_{[t_{i},t_{i+1}]\in\pi}\big(A_{t_{i}}B_{t_{i}}\eta(t_{i+1},X_{t_{i}})-A_{t_{i}}B_{t_{i}}\eta(t_{i},X_{t_{i}})\big)\right\}_{\pi\in\Pi}
, where
\Pi
is the collection of all partitions on
[t,T]
, is uniformly integrable. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}A_{r}B_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\bigg[\lim_{|\pi|\downarrow 0}\sum_{[t_{i},t_{i+1}]\in\pi}A_{t_{i}}B_{t_{i}}\big(\eta(t_{i+1},X_{t_{i}})-\eta(t_{i},X_{t_{i}})\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\lim_{|\pi|\downarrow 0}\mathbb{E}_{t}\bigg[\sum_{[t_{i},t_{i+1}]\in\pi}A_{t_{i}}B_{t_{i}}\big(\eta(t_{i+1},X_{t_{i}})-\eta(t_{i},X_{t_{i}})\big)\bigg].

Similarly, by replacing
A_{r}
with
\mathbb{E}_{r}[A_{r}]
and using (
A.2
), we can obtain that

\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{r}\left[A_{r}\right]B_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\right]=\lim_{|\pi|\downarrow 0}\mathbb{E}_{t}\bigg[\sum_{[t_{i},t_{i+1}]\in\pi}\mathbb{E}_{t_{i}}\left[A_{t_{i}}\right]B_{t_{i}}\big(\eta(t_{i+1},X_{t_{i}})-\eta(t_{i},X_{t_{i}})\big)\bigg].

The desired equation (
A.4
) follows from the last two equations and the tower rule. ∎

If we replace the condition
\|\eta\|_{\tau,\lambda}<\infty
in Lemma
A.1
by
\|\eta\|_{\tau,\lambda;\beta}<\infty
, we have the following result, of which the proof is similar to that of Lemma
A.1
and hence is omitted.

Lemma A.2
.

Assume
(H0)
holds and
\eta\in C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
for some
\beta\geq 0
. Let
A:\Omega\times[t,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a continuous process with finite p-variation a.s. such that
\mathbb{E}\left[|A_{r}|\right]<\infty
for every
r\in[t,T]
.
Assume that the process
(\omega,r)\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{r}\left[A_{r}\right](\omega)
has a continuous modification that has finite p-variation a.s. Let
X:\Omega\times[t,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
B:\Omega\times[t,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be two continuous adapted processes with finite p-variation a.s. such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1+\|X\|_{\infty;[t,T]}+\|X\|_{p\text{-}\mathrm{var};[t,T]}\right)^{\lambda+\beta}\left(\|AB\|_{\infty;[t,T]}+\|AB\|_{p\text{-}\mathrm{var};[t,T]}\right)\right]<\infty.

In addition, let
C_{r}:=\mathbb{E}_{r}[A_{r}]B_{r}
and assume

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1+\|X\|_{\infty;[t,T]}+\|X\|_{p\text{-}\mathrm{var};[t,T]}\right)^{\lambda+\beta}\left(\|C\|_{\infty;[t,T]}+\|C\|_{p\text{-}\mathrm{var};[t,T]}\right)\right]<\infty.

Then (
A.3
) and (
A.4
) hold.

Appendix B
SDEs and BSDEs on bounded domains

Throughout this section, we assume that the coefficient functions
b(\cdot)
and
\sigma(\cdot)
in Eq. (
2.1
) are independent of the time variable
t
and satisfy Assumption
(A0)
.

Let
D
be a bounded open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, with
\bar{D}
being its closure and
\partial D
its boundary. For a point
x\in\bar{D}
and an
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable random vector
\zeta\in\bar{D}
, denote by
X^{t,x}
(resp.
X^{t,\zeta}
) the unique solution to Eq. (
2.79
) with the initial value
x
(resp.
\zeta
). Denote

(B.1)

T^{t,x}:=\inf\left\{s>t;\ X^{t,x}_{s}\notin\bar{D}\right\}\land T\ \text{ and }\ T^{t,\zeta}:=\inf\left\{s>t;\ X^{t,\zeta}_{s}\notin\bar{D}\right\}\land T.

Lemma B.1
.

Suppose the set

\Lambda:=\left\{x\in\partial D;\mathbb{P}\left\{T^{0,x}>0\right\}=0\right\}
of regular points is closed. Let
\{\zeta_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}
be a sequence of
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable random vectors taking values in
\bar{D}
converging to
\zeta\in\bar{D}
in probability as
m\to\infty
. Then,

T^{t,\zeta_{m}}\rightarrow T^{t,\zeta}
in probability as
m\to\infty
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[
46
, Proposition 4.1]
, hence is omitted.
∎

Consider the following BSDEs with random terminal times,

(B.2)

\left\{\begin{aligned} dY_{s}^{t,x}=&-f\left(s,X_{s}^{t,x},Y_{s}^{t,x},Z_{s}^{t,x}\right)ds+Z_{s}^{t,x}dW_{s},\ s\in[t,T^{t,x}],\\
Y^{t,x}_{T^{t,x}}=&h\left(X_{T^{t,x}}^{t,x}\right);\\
d\mathscr{Y}_{s}^{t,\zeta}=&-f\left(s,X_{s}^{t,\zeta},\mathscr{Y}_{s}^{t,\zeta},\mathscr{Z}_{s}^{t,\zeta}\right)ds+\mathscr{Z}_{s}^{t,\zeta}dW_{s},\ s\in[t,T^{t,\zeta}],\\
\mathscr{Y}_{T^{t,\zeta}}^{t,\zeta}=&h\left(X_{T^{t,\zeta}}^{t,\zeta}\right),\end{aligned}\right.

where
x\in\bar{D}
is a point,
\zeta\in\bar{D}
is an
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable random vector, and
T^{t,x}
and
T^{t,\zeta}
are defined in (
B.1
). Assume
h\in C^{\text{Lip}}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{N})
and
f
satisfies (
2.2
) in
(A1)
with
\lambda\in(0,1],

\beta\geq 0,\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Then, by Theorem 4.2.1, Theorem 4.3.1, and Remark 4.3.2 in

[
50
]
, each BSDE in Eq. (
B.2
) admits a unique solution in
\mathfrak{H}^{2}(t,T)
(recall that
\mathfrak{H}^{2}(t,T)
is defined in (
3.13
)). Below, we prove the Markov property for the process
Y^{t}
in Eq. (
B.2
) (see also
[
50
, Theorem 5.1.3]
for the Markov property for BSDEs with deterministic terminal time).

Lemma B.2
.

Assume the set

\Lambda:=\left\{x\in\partial D;\ \mathbb{P}\left\{T^{0,x}>0\right\}=0\right\}
of regular points is closed.
Then, for every fixed
t\in[0,T]
and
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable random variable
\zeta\in\bar{D}
, we have for
s\in[t,T]
,

(B.3)

\mathscr{Y}_{s}^{t,\zeta}(\omega)=Y_{s}^{t,\zeta(\omega)}(\omega):=Y_{s}^{t,x}(\omega)|_{x=\zeta(\omega)}\text{ a.s.},

where
(x,s,\omega)\mapsto Y^{t,x}_{s}(\omega)
is a progressively measurable version of
Y^{t,x}_{s}
for each
x\in\bar{D}
(which is still denoted by
Y^{t,x}_{s}
). Consequently,
Y^{t,x}_{\cdot}
is Markov in the sense that for any bounded Borel measurable function
\varphi
and
t\leq s\leq r\leq T
,
\mathbb{E}_{s}[\varphi(Y^{t,x}_{r})]=\mathbb{E}[\varphi(Y^{s,y}_{r})]\big|_{y=\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}
, where
\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}:=X^{t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}}.
In particular, we have
Y_{s}^{t,x}=u(s,\bar{X}_{s}^{t,x})
a.s., where
u(s,x)=Y_{s}^{s,x}
.

Proof.

Firstly, we assume that
\zeta(\omega)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{n}\bm{1}_{A_{n}}(\omega)
takes countably many values, where
a_{n}\in\bar{D}
is a constant and
\{A_{n}\}_{n}\subset\mathcal{F}_{t}
is a partition of
\Omega
. Then we have, noting
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\bm{1}_{A_{n}}(\omega)\equiv 1
,

\displaystyle Y^{t,\zeta(\omega)}_{s}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\bm{1}_{A_{n}}(\omega)\cdot Y^{t,a_{n}}_{s}(\omega)

\displaystyle=h(X^{t,\zeta(\omega)}_{T^{t,\zeta(\omega)}(\omega)}(\omega))+\int_{s\land T^{t,\zeta(\omega)}(\omega)}^{T^{t,\zeta(\omega)}(\omega)}f(r,X^{t,\zeta(\omega)}_{r}(\omega),Y^{t,\zeta(\omega)}_{r}(\omega),\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(\bm{1}_{A_{n}}Z^{t,a_{n}}_{r}(\omega)))dr

\displaystyle-\int_{s\land T^{t,\zeta}}^{T^{t,\zeta}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(\bm{1}_{A_{n}}Z^{t,a_{n}}_{r})dW_{r}(\omega),

and

\int_{t}^{T}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big|\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(\bm{1}_{A_{n}}Z^{t,a_{n}}_{r})\Big|^{2}\bigg]dr\leq\sup_{x\in\bar{D}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\left|Z^{t,x}_{r}\right|^{2}dr\right]<\infty.

Therefore,
\big(Y^{t,\zeta}_{s},\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(\bm{1}_{A_{n}}Z^{t,a_{n}}_{s})\big)
solves the second equation in (
B.2
), and hence
\mathscr{Y}_{s}^{t,\zeta}=Y_{s}^{t,\zeta}
a.s.

To deal with the general situation, we claim that

(B.4)

\left\|\left\|\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}-\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta}\right\|_{\infty;[t,T]}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\rightarrow 0,\text{ if }\left\|\zeta^{m}-\zeta\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\rightarrow 0\text{, as }m\rightarrow\infty,

which will be proved afterwards. For an
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable random vector
\zeta\in\bar{D}
, let
\zeta^{m}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
take countably many values in
\bar{D}
such that
\left\|\zeta^{m}-\zeta\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\rightarrow 0
as
m\rightarrow\infty.

Then, it follows from (
B.4
) that

(B.5)

\lim_{m\to\infty}\left\|\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{s}-\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta}_{s}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}=0.

Moreover,

\mathbb{E}\left[\big|Y^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{s}-Y^{t,\zeta}_{s}\big|^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\left|Y^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{s}-Y^{t,\zeta}_{s}\right|^{2}\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Y^{t,x^{\prime}}_{s}-Y^{t,x}_{s}\right|^{2}\right]\right|_{x^{\prime}=\zeta^{m},x=\zeta}\right],

where we use the fact that
\left|Y^{t,x^{\prime}}_{s}-Y^{t,x}_{s}\right|^{2}\in\mathcal{F}^{t}_{T}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{t}
. By (
B.4
) again, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Y^{t,x^{\prime}}_{s}-Y^{t,x}_{s}\right|^{2}\right]\rightarrow 0,\text{ whenever }x^{\prime}\rightarrow x.

Noting that (see
[
50
, Theorem 4.2.1]
)

\sup_{x\in\bar{D}}\|Y^{t,x}_{s}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\sup_{x\in\bar{D}}\|\|Y^{t,x}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[t,T]}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}<\infty,

we can apply dominated convergence theorem and get

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Y^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{s}-Y^{t,\zeta}_{s}\right|^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Y^{t,x^{\prime}}_{s}-Y^{t,x}_{s}\right|^{2}\right]\right|_{x^{\prime}=\zeta^{m},x=\zeta}\right]\rightarrow 0,\ \text{as }m\rightarrow\infty.

Noting
\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta_{m}}_{s}=Y^{t,\zeta_{m}}_{s}
and combining the above convergence with (
B.5
), we get
\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta}_{s}=Y^{t,\zeta}_{s}
, which proves (
B.3
). This implies the Markov property. Indeed, by the uniqueness of the solution, for
t\leq s<r\leq T
we have

(B.6)

Y^{t,x}_{r}=\mathscr{Y}^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}_{r}=Y^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}_{r}.

Noting that
Y_{\cdot}^{s,y}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{s}
, we have for any bounded Borel measurable function
\varphi
,

\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[\varphi(Y^{t,x}_{r})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi(Y^{s,y}_{r})\right]\big|_{y=\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}.

We have proven our results assuming (
B.4
). We now prove (
B.4
), to complete the proof. Recall that
\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}
solves Eq. (
B.2
) with
\zeta
replaced by
\zeta^{m}
. Denote
(\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m},\delta\mathscr{Z}^{m}):=(\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}-\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}}-\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta}).
Then, Itô’s formula yields, for
s\in[t,T]
,

(B.7)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{s}|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{s}^{T}|\delta\mathscr{Z}^{m}_{r}|^{2}dr\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{s}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|\left|f(r,X^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r})-f(r,X^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta}_{r})\right|\right]dr

\displaystyle+2\int_{s}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|\left|f(r,X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r})-f(r,X^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r})\right|\right]dr

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{T^{t,\zeta}\land T^{t,\zeta^{m}}}^{T^{t,\zeta}\vee T^{t,\zeta^{m}}}|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|\left(\left|f(r,X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r})\right|+\left|f(r,X^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta}_{r})\right|\right)dr\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|h(X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{T^{t,\zeta^{m}}})-h(X^{t,\zeta}_{T^{t,\zeta}})\right|^{2}\right].

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
B.7
), by condition (
2.2
) and the Young’s inequality (
2|a||b|\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
), we get

(B.8)

\displaystyle 2\int_{s}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|\left|f(r,X^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r})-f(r,X^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta}_{r})\right|\right]dr

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{\text{Lip}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{s}^{T}|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|^{2}+|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|\cdot|\delta\mathscr{Z}^{m}_{r}|dr\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{\text{Lip}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{s}^{T}|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|^{2}dr\right]+2|C_{\text{Lip}}|^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{s}^{T}|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|^{2}dr\right]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{s}^{T}|\delta\mathscr{Z}^{m}_{r}|^{2}dr\right].

For the second term, in view of the local Hölder continuity of
x\mapsto f(t,x,y,z)
assumed in
(A1)
, by the inequality
\|\|X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{\cdot}-X^{t,\zeta}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[t,T]}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|\zeta^{m}-\zeta\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
(see
[
50
, Theorem 3.2.4]
),
it follows that

(B.9)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|\left|f(r,X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r})-f(r,X^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r})\right|\right]dr

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|^{2}\right]dr+\int_{s}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r}-X^{t,\zeta}_{r}|^{\mu}(1+|X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r}|^{\mu^{\prime}+\frac{\mu}{2}}+|X^{t,\zeta}_{r}|^{\mu^{\prime}+\frac{\mu}{2}})^{2}\right]dr

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|^{2}\right]dr+\|\zeta^{m}-\zeta\|^{\mu}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

For the third term, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{T^{t,\zeta}\land T^{t,\zeta^{m}}}^{T^{t,\zeta}\vee T^{t,\zeta^{m}}}|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|\left(\left|f(r,X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r})\right|+\left|f(r,X^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta}_{r})\right|\right)dr\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(1+\|(\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta})\|^{2}_{\mathfrak{H}^{4}(t,T)}+\|(\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}})\|^{2}_{\mathfrak{H}^{4}(t,T)}\Big)\|T^{t,\zeta}-T^{t,\zeta^{m}}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

(B.10)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|T^{t,\zeta}-T^{t,\zeta^{m}}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)},

where the last inequality follows from

[
48
, Proposition 4.2]
.

For the last term on the right-hand side of (
B.7
), by
[
50
, Theorems 5.2.1 and 5.2.2]
we have

(B.11)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|h(X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{T^{t,\zeta^{m}}})-h(X^{t,\zeta}_{T^{t,\zeta}})\right|^{2}\right]\lesssim\|T^{t,\zeta}-T^{t,\zeta^{m}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}+\|\zeta^{m}-\zeta\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Combining the estimates (
B.7
)-(
B.11
), and applying Hölder’s inequality and Gronwall’s inequality, we obtain, for
s\in[t,T]
,

(B.12)

\mathbb{E}\left[|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{s}|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{s}^{T}|\delta\mathscr{Z}^{m}_{r}|^{2}dr\right]\lesssim\|\zeta^{m}-\zeta\|^{\mu}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|T^{t,\zeta}-T^{t,\zeta^{m}}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Furthermore, by (
B.2
) and the BDG inequality, we have

(B.13)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}\|^{2}_{\infty;[t,T]}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{t}^{T}\left|f(r,X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r})-f(r,X^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta}_{r})\right|dr\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{T^{t,\zeta}\land T^{t,\zeta^{m}}}^{T^{t,\zeta}\vee T^{t,\zeta^{m}}}\left(\left|f(r,X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{r})\right|+\left|f(r,X^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Y}^{t,\zeta}_{r},\mathscr{Z}^{t,\zeta}_{r})\right|\right)dr\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}|\delta\mathscr{Z}^{m}_{r}|^{2}dr\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|h(X^{t,\zeta^{m}}_{T^{t,\zeta^{m}}})-h(X^{t,\zeta}_{T^{t,\zeta}})\right|^{2}\right].

Then by a similar analysis leading to (
B.12
), from (
B.13
) we get

(B.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}\|^{2}_{\infty;[t,T]}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\delta\mathscr{Y}^{m}_{r}|^{2}\right]dr+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}|\delta\mathscr{Z}^{m}_{r}|^{2}dr\right]+\|\zeta^{m}-\zeta\|^{\mu}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|T^{t,\zeta}-T^{t,\zeta^{m}}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Then, the desired (
B.4
) follows from (
B.12
), (
B.14
), and Lemma
B.1
, and the proof is concluded.
∎

Since the coefficient functions
f
and
h
in Eq. (
B.2
) are deterministic,
Y^{t,x}_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}^{t}
is a constant a.s. We denote

u(t,x):=Y^{t,x}_{t},~(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\bar{D}.

Lemma B.3
.

Under the same conditions as
in Lemma
B.2
, the function
(t,x)\mapsto u(t,x)
is continuous on
[0,T]\times\bar{D}
.

Proof.

Let
T^{t,\zeta}
be given in (
B.1
) and denote
S^{t,\zeta}:=\inf\{r>t;\ X^{t,\zeta}_{r}\notin\bar{D}\}
. Denote

\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}:=X^{t,x}_{s\land T^{t,x}}.

Since
(X^{t,x},X^{t,y})^{\top}
solves a time-homogeneous SDE for which the pathwise uniqueness holds, by the Yamada-Watanabe theorem (see, e.g.,
[
36
, Proposition 5.3.20]
),
(X^{t,x},X^{t,y})^{\top}
has the same distribution as
(X^{0,x},X^{0,y})^{\top}
. Therefore,
for
x,y\in\bar{D}
,
0\leq t<s<T
, and
\delta\in(s-t,T-t]
, we have that

\left(S^{s,y}\land T\right)-\left(S^{s,x}\land T\right)\overset{\text{d}}{=}\left(S^{0,y}\land(T-s)\right)-\left(S^{0,x}\land(T-s)\right),

where the symbol “
\overset{\text{d}}{=}
” denotes equality in distribution.
Similarly, we also have

\left(S^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}\land T\right)-\left(S^{s,x}\land T\right)\overset{\text{d}}{=}\left(S^{\delta,\bar{X}^{\delta-s+t,x}_{\delta}}\land(\delta+T-s)\right)-\left(S^{\delta,x}\land(\delta+T-s)\right).

Noting that
T^{t,\zeta}=S^{t,\zeta}\wedge T
and that for
s\in(t,t+\delta)\subset[0,T]
,

\begin{cases}\left|\big(a\land(T-s)\big)-\big(b\land(T-s)\big)\right|\leq\left|\big(a\land T\big)-\big(b\land T\big)\right|,\\
\left|\big(a\land(\delta+T-s)\big)-\big(b\land(\delta+T-s)\big)\right|\leq\left|\big(a\land 2T\big)-\big(b\land 2T\big)\right|,\end{cases}

we get

(B.15)

\mathbb{E}\left[\big|T^{s,y}-T^{s,x}\big|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\big|T^{0,y}-T^{0,x}\big|^{2}\right]

and

(B.16)

\mathbb{E}\left[\big|T^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}-T^{s,x}\big|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\big|(S^{\delta,\bar{X}^{\delta-s+t,x}_{\delta}}\land 2T)-(S^{\delta,x}\land 2T)\big|^{2}\right].

On the other hand, we claim that
\bar{X}^{\delta-s+t,x}_{\delta}\rightarrow x
in probability as
s\downarrow t
. Indeed, by
[
50
, Theorem 5.2.2]
,

(B.17)

\displaystyle\|\bar{X}^{\delta-s+t,x}_{\delta}-x\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\left\|\|X^{\delta-s+t,x}_{\cdot}-x\|_{\infty;[\delta-s+t,\delta]}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim s-t,

which implies our claim. Thus by Lemma
B.1
, we have

(B.18)

\mathbb{E}\left[\big|T^{0,y}-T^{0,x}\big|^{2}\right]\to 0,\ \text{ as }y\to x,

(B.19)

\mathbb{E}\left[\big|(S^{\delta,\bar{X}^{\delta-s+t,x}_{\delta}}\land 2T)-(S^{\delta,x}\land 2T)\big|^{2}\right]\to 0,\ \text{ as }s\downarrow t.

Then, (
B.15
) and (
B.18
) imply

(B.20)

\sup_{s\in(t,t+\delta)}\mathbb{E}\left[\big|T^{s,y}-T^{s,x}\big|^{2}\right]\to 0,\ \text{ if }y\rightarrow x,

while (
B.16
) and (
B.19
) yield

(B.21)

\mathbb{E}\left[\big|T^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}-T^{s,x}\big|^{2}\right]\to 0,\text{ as }s\downarrow t.

Now, we are ready to prove the continuity of
u.
By (
B.6
), we have
Y^{t,x}_{s}=Y^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}_{s}
. Then we have

|u(t,x)-u(s,x)|\leq\left|\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{t,x}_{t}-Y^{t,x}_{s}\right]\right|+\mathbb{E}\left[\big|Y^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}_{s}-Y^{s,x}_{s}\big|\right].

By the equality

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{t,x}_{t}-Y^{t,x}_{s}\right]\right|=\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t}^{s\land T^{t,x}}f(r,X^{t,x}_{r},Y^{t,x}_{r},Z^{t,x}_{r})dr\Big]\bigg|,

we obtain that
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{t,x}_{t}-Y^{t,x}_{s}\right]\right|\lesssim|t-s|^{1/2}
. Meanwhile, by (
B.12
) and
[
50
, Theorem 5.2.2]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\big|Y^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}_{s}-Y^{s,x}_{s}\big|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}-x\|^{\mu}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|T^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}-T^{s,x}\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq|t-s|^{\frac{\mu}{2}}+\|T^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}-T^{s,x}\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Thus, we have

(B.22)

|u(t,x)-u(s,x)|\lesssim|t-s|^{\frac{\mu}{2}}+\|T^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}-T^{s,x}\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

In a similar way, we can also get

(B.23)

|u(s,x)-u(s,y)|\lesssim|x-y|^{\mu}+\|T^{s,x}-T^{s,y}\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Noting (
B.22
) and (
B.23
), by the triangular inequality we get

(B.24)

|u(t,x)-u(s,y)|\lesssim|t-s|^{\frac{\mu}{2}}+|x-y|^{\mu}+\|T^{s,x}-T^{s,y}\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|T^{s,\bar{X}^{t,x}_{s}}-T^{s,x}\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Combining (
B.24
) with (
B.20
), (
B.21
) and (
B.17
), we get

\lim\limits_{s\downarrow t,y\rightarrow x}u(s,y)=u(t,x).

It remains to show the left continuity in time. In view of the triangular inequality

|u(t,x)-u(s,y)|\leq|u(t,x)-u(t,y)|+|u(t,y)-u(s,y)|,

and the above argument, we have

|u(t,x)-u(s,y)|\lesssim|t-s|^{\frac{\mu}{2}}+|x-y|^{\mu}+\|T^{t,x}-T^{t,y}\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|T^{s,\bar{X}^{t,y}_{s}}-T^{s,y}\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}(\Omega)},

which yields

\lim\limits_{t\uparrow s,x\rightarrow y}u(t,x)=u(s,y).

∎

Appendix C
The proof of Theorem
1
for the general case

The proof of Theorem
1
for general cases when
N=1
and
f\equiv 0
is not trivial. Indeed, there are two technical problems. First, the condition
f\neq 0
may yield an additional drift term in Eq. (
2.32
), which forces
A^{t;n}_{s}
in (
2.33
) to solve a Young SDE rather than a Young ODE. Second, the dimension
N>1
makes the arguments in (
2.46
)–(
2.47
) fail, since
|A^{t;n}_{s}|^{k/(k-1)}
cannot be represented by an exponential function in general.

We first give two lemmas that will be useful for our proof. Denote

\mathrm{H}^{2}:=\left\{A:A\text{ is a continuous, adapted process such that }\left\|\|A\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}<\infty\right\},

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\text{loc}}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{A:\text{there exists a sequence of increasing stopping times }

\displaystyle\{S_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}\text{ such that }A_{r}\mathbf{1}_{[0,S_{m}]}(r)\in\mathrm{H}^{2}\text{ and }\lim_{m}\mathbb{P}\{S_{m}=T\}=1\}\Big\}.

Lemma C.1
.

Suppose Assumption
(A0)
holds, and
v\geq 1
is an integer. Assume
\eta\in C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
for some
(\tau,\lambda,\beta)\in(0,1]\times(0,1]\times[0,\infty)
, where
\partial_{t}\eta_{i}(t,x)
exists and is continuous in
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
; and assume
\alpha^{i},\iota,c:[0,T]\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{v\times v}
are bounded adapted processes,
i=1,2,...,M
. Let
S
be a stopping time with
S\leq T
. Then the following stochastic differential equation (SDE) admits a unique solution
A^{S}\in\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}

(C.1)

A^{S}_{s}=I_{v}+\sum_{i=1}^{M}\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(\alpha^{i}_{r})^{\top}A^{S}_{r}\partial_{r}\eta_{i}(r,X_{r})dr+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(\iota_{r})^{\top}A^{S}_{r}dr+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(c_{r})^{\top}A^{S}_{r}dW_{r},\ s\in[0,T].

In addition, for stopping time
S^{\prime}
with
S\leq S^{\prime}\leq T,
we have a.s.,
A^{S^{\prime}}_{t}A^{S}_{S^{\prime}}=A^{S}_{t}
holds for all
t\in[S^{\prime},T]
.

Proof.

Let
T_{n}
be given by (
2.6
). Since the processes
\alpha^{i}_{t}\partial_{t}\eta_{i}(t,X_{t})\mathbf{1}_{[S\land T_{n},T_{n}]}(t),

\iota_{t}\mathbf{1}_{[S\land T_{n},T_{n}]}(t),
and
c_{t}\mathbf{1}_{[S\land T_{n},T_{n}]}(t)
are all bounded, the parameters of Eq. (
C.1
) are Lipschitz on
[0,T_{n}]
. Therefore, the equation admits a unique solution in
\mathrm{H}^{2}
on
[0,T_{n}]
(see
[
45
, Theorem 5.2.1]
). Thus, by the equality
\lim_{n}\mathbb{P}\left\{T_{n}=T\right\}=1
and the localization argument, the uniqueness of Eq. (
C.1
) in
\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\text{loc}}
holds on the entire interval
[0,T]
.

The second assertion can be proved in the same way as for Lemme
2.5
, and hence we omit it.
∎

For integers
v,j\geq 1,
and matrix
\Lambda\in\mathbb{R}^{v^{2^{j}}\times v^{2^{j}}}
, let
I^{\prime}(\Lambda):=\big((\Lambda e^{v;j}_{1})^{\top},...,\Lambda e^{v;j}_{v^{2^{j}}}\big)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{v^{2^{j}}\times 1}
,

\tilde{I}_{v^{2}\times 1}:=I^{\prime}(I_{v}),\quad\tilde{I}_{v^{2^{j+1}}\times 1}:=I^{\prime}(\tilde{I}_{v^{2^{j}}\times 1}\tilde{I}_{v^{2^{j}}\times 1}^{\top}),

where
\{e^{v;j}_{1},...,e^{v;j}_{v^{2^{j}}}\}
is the coordinate system of
\mathbb{R}^{v^{2^{j}}}
, and
I^{\prime}:\mathbb{R}^{v^{2^{j}}\times v^{2^{j}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{v^{2^{j+1}}\times 1}.

Next, we will present a lemma to bound the higher-order moments of the solution to a matrix-valued SDE using the expectation of the solution to a vector-valued SDE.

Lemma C.2
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
C.1
, assume further that
m_{p,2}(\alpha^{i};[0,T])<\infty,

i=1,2,...,M
. Denote by
A^{S}
the unique solution of the SDE (
C.1
) with a stopping time
S
satisfying
S\leq T
. Then for any
j\geq 1
, there exist progressively measurable processes
\tilde{\alpha}^{i},

\tilde{\iota},

\tilde{c}:[0,T]\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{v^{2^{j}}\times v^{2^{j}}},
and a constant
C>0
such that

(C.2)

|\tilde{\alpha}^{i}_{t}|\leq C|\alpha^{i}_{t}|,\ |\tilde{\iota}_{t}|\leq C|\iota_{t}|,\ |\tilde{c}_{t}|\leq C|c_{t}|;

(C.3)

m_{p,k}(\tilde{\alpha}^{i};[0,T])\leq C\cdot m_{p,k}(\alpha^{i};[0,T]);

and for any stopping time
S^{\prime}
satisfying
S\leq S^{\prime}\leq T
, it follows that

(C.4)

\mathbb{E}_{S}\left[|A^{S}_{S^{\prime}}|^{2^{j}}\right]\leq C\left|\mathbb{E}_{S}\left[\tilde{B}^{S}_{S^{\prime}}\right]\right|,

where
\tilde{B}^{S}
is the unique solution of the following
v^{2^{j}}
-dimensional vector-valued SDE

\tilde{B}^{S}_{s}=\tilde{I}_{v^{2^{j}}\times 1}+\sum_{i=1}^{M}\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(\tilde{\alpha}^{i}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{B}^{S}_{r}\partial_{r}\eta_{i}(r,X_{r})dr+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(\tilde{\iota}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{B}^{S}_{r}dr+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(\tilde{c}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{B}^{S}_{r}dW_{r}.

Proof.

Assume
M=d=1
without loss of generality. For
j=1,
set
C^{S}_{s}:=A^{S}_{s}(A^{S}_{s})^{\top}
; then it satisfies that

\displaystyle C^{S}_{s}=I_{v}

\displaystyle+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}\left((\alpha_{r})^{\top}C^{S}_{r}+C^{S}_{r}\alpha_{r}\right)\partial_{r}\eta(r,X_{r})+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}\Big((\iota_{r})^{\top}C^{S}_{r}+C^{S}_{r}\iota_{r}

\displaystyle+(c_{r})^{\top}C^{S}_{r}c_{r}\Big)dr+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}\left((c_{r})^{\top}C^{S}_{r}+C^{S}_{r}c_{r}\right)dW_{r},\ s\in[0,T].

Let
\tilde{C}^{S}_{s}:=I^{\prime}(C^{S}_{s})
. Similar to the proof of
[
48
, Proposition 3.5]
, it can be shown that there exist processes
\alpha^{\prime}_{r},\iota^{\prime}_{r},c^{\prime}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{v^{2}\times v^{2}}
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

Each entry of
\alpha^{\prime}_{r}
(resp.
c^{\prime}_{r}
) is a linear combination of the elements of
\alpha_{r}
(resp.
c_{r}
), and each entry of
\iota^{\prime}_{r}
is a linear combination of the elements of
(\iota_{r},c_{r}).

(2)

For
i,i^{\prime}=1,2,...,v,
the process
\alpha^{\prime}
satisfies that for every
\Lambda\in\mathbb{R}^{v\times v}
,

(\underbrace{\overbrace{0,\ 0,\ \cdots,\ 0}^{(i^{\prime}-1)v},\ e^{\top}_{i},}_{i^{\prime}v}\ 0,\ \cdots,\ 0)_{1\times v^{2}}\cdot(\alpha^{\prime}_{r})^{\top}I^{\prime}(\Lambda)=\left((\alpha_{r})^{\top}\Lambda+\Lambda\alpha_{r}\right)_{ii^{\prime}},

and
c^{\prime}
satisfies the above equality with
(\alpha_{r},\alpha^{\prime}_{r})
replaced by
(c_{r},c^{\prime}_{r}).

(3)

For
i,i^{\prime}=1,2,...,v,
the process
\iota^{\prime}
satisfies that for every
\Lambda\in\mathbb{R}^{v\times v}
,

(\underbrace{\overbrace{0,\ 0,\ \cdots,\ 0}^{(i^{\prime}-1)v},\ e^{\top}_{i},}_{i^{\prime}v}\ 0,\ \cdots,\ 0)_{1\times v^{2}}\cdot(\iota^{\prime}_{r})^{\top}I^{\prime}(\Lambda)=\left((\iota_{r})^{\top}\Lambda+\Lambda\iota_{r}+(c_{r})^{\top}\Lambda c_{r}\right)_{ii^{\prime}}.

Then,
\tilde{C}^{S}_{s}
uniquely solves the following
v^{2}
-dimensional SDE,

\tilde{C}^{S}_{s}=\tilde{I}_{v^{2}\times 1}+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(\alpha^{\prime}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{C}^{S}_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(\iota^{\prime}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{C}^{S}_{r}dr+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(c^{\prime}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{C}^{S}_{r}dW_{r},\ s\in[0,T].

By condition (1), both (
C.2
) and (
C.3
) hold with
(\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\iota},\tilde{c})
replaced by
(\alpha^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},c^{\prime})
. Furthermore, (
C.4
) follows from the following fact, with
j=1
, and
\tilde{B}^{S}
replaced by
\tilde{C}^{S}
,

\mathbb{E}_{S}\left[|A^{S}_{S^{\prime}}|^{2}\right]=\text{tr}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{S}\left[C^{S}_{S^{\prime}}\right]\right\}\lesssim\left|\mathbb{E}_{S}\left[C^{S}_{S^{\prime}}\right]\right|=\big|\mathbb{E}_{S}\big[\tilde{C}^{S}_{S^{\prime}}\big]\big|.

For
j=2
, let
D^{S}_{s}:=\tilde{C}^{S}_{s}(\tilde{C}^{S}_{s})^{\top},
then
D^{S}
satisfies

\displaystyle D^{S}_{s}=\tilde{I}_{v^{2}\times 1}(\tilde{I}_{v^{2}\times 1})^{\top}

\displaystyle+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}\left((\alpha^{\prime}_{r})^{\top}D^{S}_{r}+D^{S}_{r}\alpha^{\prime}_{r}\right)\partial_{r}\eta(r,X_{r})dr+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}\Big((\iota^{\prime}_{r})^{\top}D^{S}_{r}+D^{S}_{r}\iota^{\prime}_{r}

\displaystyle+(c^{\prime}_{r})^{\top}D^{S}_{r}c^{\prime}_{r}\Big)dr+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}\left((c^{\prime}_{r})^{\top}D^{S}_{r}+D^{S}_{r}c^{\prime}_{r}\right)dW_{r},\ s\in[0,T].

Let
\tilde{D}^{S}_{s}:=I^{\prime}(D^{S}_{s})
. In view of the inequality
|A^{S}_{S^{\prime}}|^{4}\lesssim|\tilde{C}^{S}_{S^{\prime}}|^{2}
, by repeating the procedure for
j=1
, there exists
(\alpha^{\star},\iota^{\star},c^{\star})
so that (
C.2
)–(
C.4
) hold with
j=2
and
(\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\iota},\tilde{c},\alpha,\iota,c,\tilde{B}^{S})
replaced by
(\alpha^{\star},\iota^{\star},c^{\star},\alpha^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},c^{\prime},\tilde{D}^{S})
. By iterating the same argument, the case for
j\geq 3
can be proved.
∎

Now we give the full proof of Theorem
1
. Let
\Theta_{2}:=(\tau,\lambda,\beta,p,k,l,\varepsilon,T,C_{1},C_{\text{Lip}},L,\|\eta\|_{\tau,\lambda;\beta})
be as defined in Section
2.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Assume
M=d=1
without loss of generality. First, we additionally assume that
\eta
is smooth in time and that
\partial_{t}\eta(t,x)
is continuous in
(t,x)
. The general cases will be proved at the end of the proof. By
[
48
, Proposition 4.1]
, for
n\geq 1
, the following BSDE admits a unique solution in
\bigcap_{q>1}\mathfrak{B}_{p,q}(0,T)
,

\begin{cases}dY_{t}=-f^{n}_{t}dt-g(Y_{t})\partial_{t}\eta(t,X_{t})dt+Z_{t}dW_{t},\ t\in[0,T_{n}],\\
Y_{T_{n}}=\Xi_{T_{n}},\end{cases}

where
f^{n}_{t}:=f(t,X_{t},Y_{t},Z_{t})
, and we denote the solution by
(Y^{n},Z^{n})
. For
i=1,2,...,N
, denote
Y^{n}_{t}=(Y^{n;1}_{t},Y^{n;2}_{t},...,Y^{n;N}_{t})^{\top},

Z^{n}_{t}=(Z^{n;1}_{t},Z^{n;2}_{t},...,Z^{n;N}_{t})^{\top},

\delta Y^{n}_{t}:=Y^{n+1}_{t}-Y^{n}_{t},

\delta Z^{n}_{t}:=Z^{n+1}_{t}-Z^{n}_{t}
,

\displaystyle\delta g^{n;i}_{t}

\displaystyle:=g(\overbrace{Y^{n;1}_{t},...,Y^{n;i-1}_{t},}^{i-1}Y^{n+1;i}_{t},Y^{n+1;i+1}_{t},...,Y^{n+1;N}_{t})

\displaystyle-g(\overbrace{Y^{n;1}_{t},...,Y^{n;i-1}_{t},Y^{n;i}_{t},}^{i}Y^{n+1;i+1}_{t},...,Y^{n+1;N}_{t}),

and

\alpha^{n}_{t}:=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\delta g^{n;1}_{t}}{\delta Y^{n;1}_{t}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\delta Y^{n;1}_{t}\neq 0\}},\ \frac{\delta g^{n;2}_{t}}{\delta Y^{n;2}_{t}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\delta Y^{n;2}_{t}\neq 0\}},...,\ \frac{\delta g^{n;N}_{t}}{\delta Y^{n;N}_{t}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\delta Y^{n;N}_{t}\neq 0\}}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}.

For
z\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
i=1,2,...,N
, set

\displaystyle\delta f^{n;i}_{t}(z)

\displaystyle:=f(t,X_{t},\overbrace{Y^{n;1}_{t},...,Y^{n;i-1}_{t},}^{i-1}Y^{n+1;i}_{t},Y^{n+1;i+1}_{t},...,Y^{n+1;N}_{t},z)

\displaystyle-f(t,X_{t},\overbrace{Y^{n;1}_{t},...,Y^{n;i-1}_{t},Y^{n;i}_{t},}^{i}Y^{n+1;i+1}_{t},...,Y^{n+1;N}_{t},z),

and

\displaystyle\delta\tilde{f}^{n;i}_{t}

\displaystyle:=f^{n}(t,X_{t},Y^{n}_{t},\overbrace{Z^{n;1}_{t},...,Z^{n;i-1}_{t},}^{i-1}Z^{n+1;i}_{t},Z^{n+1;i+1}_{t},...,Z^{n+1;N}_{t})

\displaystyle-f^{n}(t,X_{t},Y^{n}_{t},\overbrace{Z^{n;1}_{t},...,Z^{n;i-1}_{t},Z^{n;i}_{t},}^{i}Z^{n+1;i+1}_{t},...,Z^{n+1;N}_{t}).

Let
(\iota^{n}_{r},c^{n}_{r})
be defined as follows,

\begin{cases}\iota^{n}_{r}:=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\delta f^{n;1}_{r}(Z^{n+1}_{r})}{\delta Y^{n;1}_{r}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\delta Y^{n;1}_{r}\neq 0\}},\ \frac{\delta f^{n;2}_{r}(Z^{n+1}_{r})}{\delta Y^{n;2}_{r}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\delta Y^{n;2}_{r}\neq 0\}},...,\ \frac{\delta f^{n;N}_{r}(Z^{n+1}_{r})}{\delta Y^{n;N}_{r}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\delta Y^{n;N}_{r}\neq 0\}}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N},\\
c^{n}_{r}:=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\delta\tilde{f}^{n;1}_{r}}{\delta Z^{n;1}_{r}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\delta Z^{n;1}_{r}\neq 0\}},\ \frac{\delta\tilde{f}^{n;2}_{r}}{\delta Z^{n;2}_{r}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\delta Z^{n;2}_{r}\neq 0\}},...,\ \frac{\delta\tilde{f}^{n;N}_{r}}{\delta Z^{n;N}_{r}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\delta Z^{n;N}_{r}\neq 0\}}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}.\end{cases}

It follows that

(C.5)

\delta Y^{n}_{t}=Y^{n+1}_{T_{n}}-\Xi_{T_{n}}+\int_{t\land T_{n}}^{T_{n}}\alpha^{n}_{r}\delta Y^{n}_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})+\int_{t\land T_{n}}^{T_{n}}\left(\iota^{n}_{r}\delta Y^{n}_{r}+c^{n}_{r}\delta Z^{n}_{r}\right)dr-\int_{t\land T_{n}}^{T_{n}}\delta Z^{n}_{r}dW_{r}.

Step 1.
For the stopping time
S\leq T,
denote by
A^{S;n}_{s}
the unique solution of the following SDE,

A^{S;n}_{s}=I_{N}+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(\alpha^{n}_{r})^{\top}A^{S;n}_{r}\partial_{r}\eta(r,X_{r})dr+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(\iota^{n}_{r})^{\top}A^{S;n}_{r}dr+\int_{S}^{s\vee S}(c^{n}_{r})^{\top}A^{S;n}_{r}dW_{r},\ s\in[0,T].

By Assumption
(A1)
,
\alpha^{n},

\iota^{n},
and
c^{n}
are uniformly bounded in
n
. In addition, by the boundedness of the first derivative of
g
, the fact
m_{p,2}(Y^{n};[0,T])<\infty
implies that
m_{p,2}(\alpha^{n};[0,T])<\infty
(following the calculation as in
[
48
, (3.41)]
). Now set
\kappa:=\lfloor\frac{\ln(k/(k-1))}{\ln 2}\rfloor+1
. By Lemma
C.2
with
(v,j,\alpha,\iota,c,A^{S})=(N,\kappa,\alpha^{n},\iota^{n},c^{n},A^{S;n})
, there exists
(\tilde{\alpha}^{n},\tilde{\iota}^{n},\tilde{c}^{n})
that satisfies (
C.2
)–(
C.3
) with
(\alpha,\iota,c)
replaced by
(\alpha^{n},\iota^{n},c^{n})
. Furthermore, denote by
\tilde{B}^{S;n}
the unique solution of the following SDE,

\tilde{B}^{S;n}_{t}=\tilde{I}_{N^{2^{\kappa}}\times 1}+\int_{S}^{t\vee S}(\tilde{\alpha}^{n}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{B}^{S;n}_{r}\partial_{r}\eta(r,X_{r})dr+\int_{S}^{t\vee S}(\tilde{\iota}^{n}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{B}^{S;n}_{r}dr+\int_{S}^{t\vee S}(\tilde{c}^{n}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{B}^{S;n}_{r}dW_{r},

where
t\in[0,T].
Then, by the fact that
2^{\kappa}\geq k/(k-1)
, and by (
C.4
) in Lemma
C.2
, we have for every stopping time
S^{\prime}
that satisfies
S\leq S^{\prime}\leq T,
the following inequalities hold,

(C.6)

\left\{\mathbb{E}_{S}\left[|A^{S;n}_{S^{\prime}}|^{\frac{k}{k-1}}\right]\right\}^{\frac{2^{\kappa}(k-1)}{k}}\leq\mathbb{E}_{S}\left[|A^{S;n}_{S^{\prime}}|^{2^{\kappa}}\right]\lesssim\left|\mathbb{E}_{S}\left[\tilde{B}^{S;n}_{S^{\prime}}\right]\right|.

Let
G^{S;n}_{t}
denote the unique solution to the following SDE, which takes values in
\mathbb{R}^{N^{2^{\kappa}}\times N^{2^{\kappa}}}
.

G^{S;n}_{t}=I_{N^{2^{\kappa}}}+\int_{S}^{t\vee S}(\tilde{\alpha}^{n}_{r})^{\top}G^{S;n}_{r}\partial_{r}\eta(r,X_{r})dr+\int_{S}^{t\vee S}(\tilde{\iota}^{n}_{r})^{\top}G^{S;n}_{r}dr+\int_{S}^{t\vee S}(\tilde{c}^{n}_{r})^{\top}G^{S;n}_{r}dW_{r}.

Then, by the uniqueness shown in Lemma
C.1
,
\tilde{B}^{S;n}_{t}=G^{S;n}_{t}\tilde{I}_{N^{2^{k}}\times 1}.
Again, by Lemma
C.2
with
(v,j,\alpha,\iota,c,A^{S})=(N^{2^{\kappa}},1,\tilde{\alpha}^{n},\tilde{\iota}^{n},\tilde{c}^{n},G^{S;n})
, there exists a triplet
(\hat{\alpha}^{n},\hat{\iota}^{n},\hat{c}^{n})
that satisfies (
C.2
)–(
C.3
) with
(\alpha,\iota,c)
replaced by
(\tilde{\alpha}^{n},\tilde{\iota}^{n},\tilde{c}^{n})
. Let
\tilde{H}^{S;n}
be the unique solution of

\tilde{H}^{S;n}_{t}=\tilde{I}_{N^{2^{\kappa+1}}\times 1}+\int_{S}^{t\vee S}(\hat{\alpha}^{n}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{H}^{S;n}_{r}\partial_{r}\eta(r,X_{r})dr+\int_{S}^{t\vee S}(\hat{\iota}^{n}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{H}^{S;n}_{r}dr+\int_{S}^{t\vee S}(\hat{c}^{n}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{H}^{S;n}_{r}dW_{r}.

It follows from (
C.4
) that, for every stopping time
S^{\prime}
such that
S\leq S^{\prime}\leq T,

(C.7)

\mathbb{E}_{S}\left[|G^{S;n}_{S^{\prime}}|^{2}\right]\lesssim\left|\mathbb{E}_{S}\left[\tilde{H}^{S;n}_{S^{\prime}}\right]\right|.

Next, we will estimate
\mathbb{E}_{t\land S}[|G^{t\land S;n}_{S}|]
for some specific stopping time
S
. By the well-posedness for linear BSDEs with bounded drivers

[
48
, Corollary 3.3]
, there exists a unique solution
(\Psi^{n}_{t},\psi^{n}_{t})
of the following linear BSDE,

(C.8)

\begin{cases}d\Psi^{n}_{t}=-\hat{\alpha}^{n}_{t}\Psi^{n}_{t}\partial_{t}\eta(t,X_{t})dt-\left[\hat{\iota}^{n}_{t}\Psi^{n}_{t}+\hat{c}^{n}_{t}\psi^{n}_{t}\right]dt+\psi^{n}_{t}dW_{t},\ t\in[0,T_{n}],\\
\Psi^{n}_{T_{n}}=I_{N^{2^{\kappa+1}}}.\end{cases}

Note that for every
t\in[0,T]
,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big|\int_{0}^{T_{n}}|(\psi^{n}_{r})^{\top}(\hat{c}^{n}_{r})^{\top}\tilde{H}^{t\land T_{n};n}_{r}|^{2}dr\Big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg]\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\tilde{H}^{t\land T_{n};n}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[0,T_{n}]}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T_{n}}|\psi^{n}_{r}|^{2}dr\right]<\infty.

Then applying Itô’s formula to
(\Psi^{n}_{\cdot})^{\top}\tilde{H}^{t\land T_{n};n}_{\cdot},
and taking conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{t\land T_{n}}[\cdot]
, we have
(\Psi^{n}_{t})^{\top}\tilde{I}_{N^{2^{\kappa+1}}\times 1}=(\Psi^{n}_{t\land T_{n}})^{\top}\tilde{I}_{N^{2^{\kappa+1}}\times 1}=\mathbb{E}_{t\land T_{n}}[\tilde{H}^{t\land T_{n};n}_{T_{n}}].
To estimate
\Psi^{n},
by the boundedness of
\hat{\iota}
and (
1.5
), we have

(C.9)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\bigg[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T_{n}}\hat{\iota}^{n}_{r}\Psi^{n}_{r}dr\Big\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[t\land T_{n},T_{n}]}\bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{t}\bigg[\Big|\int_{t\land T_{n}}^{T_{n}}|\Psi^{n}_{r}|dr\Big|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim|T-t|^{2}\left(1+m_{p,2}(\Psi^{n};[t,T])^{2}\right).

In addition, it follows from the boundedness of
\hat{c}
and the BDG inequality
[
48
, Lemma A.1]
that

(C.10)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\bigg[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T_{n}}\hat{c}^{n}_{r}\psi^{n}_{r}dr\Big\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[t\land T_{n},T_{n}]}\bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{t}\bigg[\Big|\int_{t\land T_{n}}^{T_{n}}|\psi^{n}_{r}|dr\Big|^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim|T-t|\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\int_{t\land T_{n}}^{T_{n}}|\psi^{n}_{r}|^{2}dr\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}|T-t|\mathbb{E}_{t}\bigg[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T_{n}}\psi^{n}_{r}dW_{r}\Big\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[t\land T_{n},T_{n}]}\bigg].

Furthermore, by Lemma
2.3
with

(q,f_{t},g_{t},Y_{t},Z_{t},S,\xi)=(2,\hat{\iota}^{n}_{t}\Psi^{n}_{t}+\hat{c}^{n}_{t}\psi^{n}_{t},\hat{\alpha}^{n}_{t}\Psi^{n}_{t},\Psi^{n}_{t},\psi^{n}_{t},T_{n},I_{N^{2^{\kappa+1}}}),

(C.11)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T_{n}}\psi^{n}_{r}dWr\Big\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[t\land T_{n},T_{n}]}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T_{n}}\hat{c}^{n}_{r}\psi^{n}_{r}dr\Big\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[t\land T_{n},T_{n}]}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T_{n}}\hat{\iota}^{n}_{r}\Psi^{n}_{r}dr\Big\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[t\land T_{n},T_{n}]}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T_{n}}\hat{\alpha}^{n}_{r}\Psi^{n}_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\Big\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[t\land T_{n},T_{n}]}\right].

Also, by the same calculation leading to (
2.41
), with
(\alpha^{n},\Gamma^{n})
replaced by
(\hat{\alpha}^{n},\Psi^{n}),
we have

(C.12)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[\Big\|\int_{\cdot}^{T_{n}}\hat{\alpha}^{n}_{r}\Psi^{n}_{r}\eta(dr,X_{r})\Big\|^{2}_{p\text{-var};[t\land T_{n},T_{n}]}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}|T-t|^{2\tau}n^{2(\lambda+\beta)}\left(1+m_{p,2}(\hat{\alpha}^{n};[0,T])^{2}\right)\left(1+m_{p,2}(\Psi^{n};[t,T])^{2}\right).

Moreover, by Lemma
2.4
we have for all
n\geq|x|,

m_{p,2}(Y^{n};[0,T])\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}n^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}
. Then,
by the calculation leading to (
2.34
), we have
m_{p,2}(\alpha^{n};[0,T])\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}n^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}
for all
n\geq|x|.
Noting that

m_{p,2}(\hat{\alpha}^{n};[0,T])\lesssim m_{p,2}(\tilde{\alpha}^{n};[0,T])\lesssim m_{p,2}(\alpha^{n};[0,T]),

we have
m_{p,2}(\hat{\alpha}^{n};[0,T])\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}n^{\frac{\lambda+\beta}{\varepsilon}\vee 1}.
Hence, by Eq. (
C.8
), and combining inequalities (
C.9
), (
C.10
), (
C.11
), and (
C.12
), there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
n\geq|x|,

\displaystyle m_{p,2}(\Psi^{n};[t,T])+\|\psi^{n}\|_{2\text{-BMO};[t,T]}

\displaystyle\leq C|T-t|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\psi^{n}\|_{2\text{-BMO};[t,T]}+C|T-t|^{\tau}n^{\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\varepsilon}\vee(\lambda+\beta+1)}\left(1+m_{p,2}(\Psi^{n};[t,T])\right).

Then we have
m_{p,2}(\Psi^{n};[T-\delta,T])\leq 1,
where

(C.13)

\delta:=C^{-2}\land\left((2C)^{-\frac{1}{\tau}}n^{-(\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau})}\right).

Therefore, by (
1.5
), there exists a constant
C^{\dagger}>1
such that
\esssup_{t\in[T-\delta,T],\omega\in\Omega}|\Psi^{n}_{t}|\leq C^{\dagger}.

For
0\leq i\leq\lfloor T/\delta\rfloor,
denote
T^{i}_{n}:=T_{n}\land(T-i\delta),
and denote by
(\Psi^{n;i}_{t},\psi^{n;i}_{t})
the unique solution of

\begin{cases}d\Psi^{n;i}_{t}=-\hat{\alpha}^{n}_{t}\Psi^{n;i}_{t}\partial_{t}\eta(t,X_{t})dt-(\hat{\iota}^{n}_{t}\Psi^{n;i}_{t}+\hat{c}^{n}_{t}\psi^{n;i}_{t})dt+\psi^{n;i}_{t}dW_{t},\ t\in[0,T^{i}_{n}],\\
\Psi^{n;i}_{T^{i}_{n}}=I_{N^{2^{\kappa+1}}}.\end{cases}

In the following, we always assume
n\geq|x|
. By the procedure leading to
|\Psi^{n}_{t}|\leq C^{\dagger},
we have

(C.14)

\esssup_{t\in[T-(i+1)\delta,T-i\delta],\omega\in\Omega}|\Psi^{n;i}_{t}|\leq C^{\dagger}.

In addition, by Itô’s formula again, we have
(\Psi^{n;i}_{t})^{\top}\tilde{I}_{N^{2^{\kappa+1}}\times 1}=\mathbb{E}_{t\land T^{i}_{n}}[\tilde{H}^{t\land T^{i}_{n};n}_{T^{i}_{n}}].
Then, by (
C.7
) and (
C.14
), it follows that there exits a constant
C^{\diamond}>1
such that for all
t\in[T-(i+1)\delta,T-i\delta],

(C.15)

\mathbb{E}_{t\land T^{i}_{n}}\left[|G^{t\land T^{i}_{n};n}_{T^{i}_{n}}|\right]\leq C^{\diamond}|\Psi^{n;i}_{t}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C^{\diamond}|C^{\dagger}|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now we are ready to estimate
\mathbb{E}_{t\land T_{n}}[|A^{t\land T_{n};n}_{T_{n}}|^{\frac{k}{k-1}}]
. Denote by
(\Gamma^{n;i},\gamma^{n;i})
the unique solution of

\begin{cases}d\Gamma^{n;i}_{t}=-\tilde{\alpha}^{n}_{t}\Gamma^{n;i}_{t}\partial_{t}\eta(t,X_{t})dt-(\tilde{\iota}^{n}_{t}\Gamma^{n;i}_{t}+\tilde{c}^{n}_{t}\gamma^{n;i}_{t})dt+\gamma^{n;i}_{t}dW_{t},\ t\in[0,T^{i}_{n}],\\
\Gamma^{n;i}_{T^{i}_{n}}=I_{N^{2^{\kappa}}}.\end{cases}

By Itô’s formula, we have
(\Gamma^{n;i}_{t})^{\top}=\mathbb{E}_{t\land T^{i}_{n}}[G^{t\land T^{i}_{n};n}_{T^{i}_{n}}].

Then, by (
C.6
) and the fact that
\tilde{B}^{t\land T_{n};n}_{T_{n}}=G^{t\land T_{n};n}_{T_{n}}\tilde{I}_{N^{2^{\kappa}}\times 1}
, we obtain

(C.16)

\mathbb{E}_{t\land T_{n}}\left[|A^{t\land T_{n};n}_{T_{n}}|^{\frac{k}{k-1}}\right]\lesssim|\Gamma^{n;0}_{t}|^{\frac{k}{2^{\kappa}(k-1)}}.

For
i^{\prime}\leq i
, by Lemma
C.1
, the equality
(G^{t\land T^{i}_{n};n}_{T^{i^{\prime}}_{n}})^{\top}=(G^{t\land T^{i}_{n};n}_{T^{i}_{n}})^{\top}(G^{T^{i}_{n};n}_{T^{i^{\prime}}_{n}})^{\top}
holds. Hence, by the fact that
(\Gamma^{n;i^{\prime}}_{t})^{\top}=\mathbb{E}_{t\land T^{i}_{n}}[G^{t\land T^{i}_{n};n}_{T^{i^{\prime}}_{n}}]
for
t\leq T-i\delta
(noting
t\land T^{i}_{n}=t\land T^{i^{\prime}}_{n}
), and by (
C.15
), we have

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in[T-(i+1)\delta,T-i\delta],\omega\in\Omega}\left|\Gamma^{n;i^{\prime}}_{t}\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[T-(i+1)\delta,T-i\delta]}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{t\land T^{i}_{n}}\left[(G^{t\land T^{i}_{n};n}_{T^{i^{\prime}}_{n}})^{\top}\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[T-(i+1)\delta,T-i\delta]}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{t\land T^{i}_{n}}\left[\left|(G^{t\land T^{i}_{n};n}_{T^{i}_{n}})^{\top}\right|\cdot\left|\mathbb{E}_{T^{i}_{n}}\left[(G^{T^{i}_{n};n}_{T^{i^{\prime}}_{n}})^{\top}\right]\right|\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[T-(i+1)\delta,T-i\delta]}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{t\land T^{i}_{n}}\left[\left|(G^{t\land T^{i}_{n};n}_{T^{i}_{n}})^{\top}\right|\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\cdot\|\Gamma^{n;i^{\prime}}_{T^{i}_{n}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\diamond}|C^{\dagger}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\esssup_{t\in[T-i\delta,T-(i-1)\delta],\omega\in\Omega}|\Gamma^{n;i^{\prime}}_{t}|.

Let
i^{\prime}=0.
Then, by the following inequality,

\esssup_{t\in[T-\delta,T],\omega\in\Omega}|\Gamma^{n;0}_{t}|=\sup_{t\in[T-\delta,T]}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{t\land T_{n}}\left[(G^{t\land T_{n};n}_{T_{n}})^{\top}\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C^{\diamond}|C^{\dagger}|^{\frac{1}{2}},

it follows that for all
i=0,1,...,\lfloor T/\delta\rfloor
and
t\in[T-(i+1)\delta,T-i\delta],

(C.17)

|\Gamma^{n;0}_{t}|\leq C^{\diamond}|C^{\dagger}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\esssup_{t\in[T-i\delta,T-(i-1)\delta],\omega\in\Omega}|\Gamma^{n;0}_{t}|\leq\cdots\leq|C^{\diamond}|^{\frac{T}{\delta}+1}|C^{\dagger}|^{\frac{T}{2\delta}+\frac{1}{2}}.

Finally, by (
C.13
) and (
C.17
), there exists a constant
C^{*}>0
such that for all
n\geq|x|,

(C.18)

\esssup_{t\in[0,T],\omega\in\Omega}|\Gamma^{n;0}_{t}|\leq\exp\left\{C^{*}n^{\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau}}\right\}.

Step 2.
Since
(\delta Y^{n},\delta Z^{n})
solves Eq. (
C.5
), by Itô’s formula again, we have

(C.19)

\delta Y^{n}_{t}=\mathbb{E}_{t\land T_{n}}\left[(A^{t\land T_{n};n}_{T_{n}})^{\top}(Y^{n+1}_{T_{n}}-\Xi_{T_{n}})\right].

On the other hand, by following the same procedure that led to (
2.52
), we have

\|Y^{n+1}_{T_{n}}-\Xi_{T_{n}}\|_{L^{k}(\Omega)}\leq\|\Xi_{T_{n}}-\Xi_{T_{n+1}}\|_{L^{k}(\Omega)}+\|Y^{n+1}_{T_{n}}-\Xi_{T_{n+1}}\|_{L^{k}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},x}\exp\left\{-C^{\star}n^{2}\right\},

where
C^{\star}>0
is a constant. Combining (
C.16
), (
C.18
), and the above estimate, and applying Hölder’s inequality to (
C.19
), it follows that

(C.20)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\delta Y^{n}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[0,T_{n}]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\{\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\mathbb{E}_{t\land T_{n}}\left[|Y^{n+1}_{T_{n}}-\Xi_{T_{n}}|^{k}\right]\right|\Big\}^{\frac{1}{k}}\Big\{\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{t\land T_{n}}\left[|A^{t\land T_{n};n}_{T_{n}}|^{\frac{k}{k-1}}\right]\Big\}^{\frac{k-1}{k}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2}}\|Y^{n+1}_{T_{n}}-\Xi_{T_{n}}\|_{L^{k}(\Omega)}\Big\{\esssup_{t\in[0,T],\omega\in\Omega}|\Gamma^{n;0}_{t}|\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2^{\kappa}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},x}\exp\Big\{-C^{\star}n^{2}+\frac{C^{*}}{2^{\kappa}}n^{\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau}}\Big\}.

Then the existence follows from the same procedure as in (
2.53
)–(
2.56
).

By approximation results
[
48
, Corollary 3.1 and Lemma A.3]
, the inequality

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\delta Y^{n}_{\cdot}\|_{\infty;[0,T_{n}]}\right]\lesssim_{\Theta_{2},x}\exp\Big\{-C^{\star}n^{2}+\frac{C^{*}}{2^{\kappa}}n^{\frac{(1+\varepsilon)(\lambda+\beta)}{\tau\varepsilon}\vee\frac{\lambda+\beta+1}{\tau}}\Big\}

implied by (
C.20
) still holds for general
\eta\in C^{\tau,\lambda;\beta}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{M})
. Therefore, the existence of solutions also follows.
∎
